# Round 37 of the relation-map review

Read the round-32 brief (`audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`) for scope, guarantees and the approval bar, the round-34 brief for the name policy (`audit_evidence_claims/m34/brief.md`), and the round-36 report (`audit_evidence_claims/m36/REPORT.md`). Repairs: `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md` section "Round 36". Review the latest commit on HEAD. Tests: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (expect 365). Page: `python src/app.py <port>` (not 8765).

## What changed since round 36

- `src/app.py` `_sig` no longer interprets optical-rotation descriptors. After trademark signs, compatibility folding, case, marks (accents) and invisible characters are removed, a name that contains a bracketed group (any Unicode opening/closing or initial/final quotation punctuation) holding no letter or digit is compared character for character: only spaces are dropped and every dash (category Pd, U+2212) is read as "-". Every other name keeps the round-32 rule (spaces and punctuation dropped). Stored names go through the same function, so a descriptor name matches only its own spelling (spaces, case, accents, invisible characters and dash style aside).
- `src/query.py` `_named_alone`: any sign (+, -, minus, plus-minus) in the run of brackets, signs, dashes, slashes, underscores and spaces next to a shared-key name in a question blocks the preferred-name reading.

## What to do

1. Re-run M36-B1, B2, S1 and every round-32 to round-35 finding; FIXED / NOT FIXED with exact inputs. Re-run the historical replays and the expanded source universe.
2. Attack the design as in the round-34 brief, including the two comparison modes and which mode a name falls into.
3. Check the question-linker change for regressions in the question boxes and demos.
4. Re-check guarantees A–G briefly.

Report data problems separately from code problems. Recorded, not blockers: question-box free-text leaks and the resolver's own lists (README defect 15); the over-refusals in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, synonyms and brands the map does not draw, descriptor spellings other than the stored one, two tracer drugs and preferred names written like a refused spelling; THEOPHYLLINE's upstream drug type.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m37/`, the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m37/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

# Round 32 of the relation-map review

Previous rounds: `audit_evidence_claims/m1` to `m31` (`REPORT.md`; m27 and m29 stopped early, so their `attacks/` folders are the record). All repairs are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`. Review the latest commit on HEAD. Run the tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; expect 361. Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>` on a port other than 8765 and exercise it over HTTP.

## What changed since round 31: a design change, not another word-list repair

Rounds 20 to 31 each found new wording that the question linker's product markers did not cover. The relation map no longer depends on those markers. See the section "Before round 32" in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, and `src/app.py` `Engine.explore`, `_sig`, `_latin`.

- **P32-1.** `explore` draws only when the whole input is one name. The linker must read exactly one entity, and its matched text must have the same letters, digits and symbols as the input (`_sig`: NFKC, casefold, any script). Case, spaces, punctuation and ®/™/© do not count. Anything else returns `not_one_name`, and the page asks for the name alone.
- **P32-2.** If any part of the input is refused, the whole input is refused.
- **P32-3.** A name stored for several live nodes is not drawn. The reply is `choose`: the nodes are listed and the reader clicks one, which loads it by id.
- **P32-4.** Lower-case `nct`/`chembl`/`ensg` ids are read. The whole input can be a company key or target name when `_latin(text)` equals the dictionary key, so a symbol the key would drop still blocks it (for example "Merck & Co").
- **P32-5.** This change is in the question linker, `src/query.py` `_whole_name` and the end of `_link`. A canonical key shared by several drugs is no longer decided by its spelling; only a drug's own preferred name chooses it. Inside such a key, a shorter name is not read either. Before, "medi4736" read as atezolizumab and "5-fluorouracil" as capecitabine.
- **New tests:** `tests/test_app.py::test_the_map_draws_only_a_whole_stored_name` and `test_a_key_shared_by_several_drugs_is_not_decided_by_spelling`. Older map tests that drew a centre from a longer input now go through `_page`: the map must not draw, and the question linker's reading is checked instead.
- **Docs:** README defect 15 (both languages) records that the question boxes still rely on the linker's markers for free text. Defect 9 records the shared keys. Section 0 of `web/try.html` states the one-name rule.

## Scope and approval bar

The subject is the relation map: section 0 of `web/try.html`, `POST /api/explore`, `Engine.explore`. The guarantees are those in the original brief below, with guarantee 3 now read as follows: the map draws a node only for an input that is, as a whole, one stored name of that node (or its id). It never draws a refused product, tracer or conjugate as the originator, and never draws one of several nodes that share a name.

1. Re-run every earlier reproduction from m1 to m31 against the map, and mark each finding FIXED or NOT FIXED. Reuse the earlier scripts. Inputs that were longer than a name should now be refused, `not_one_name`, `choose` or `not_found`, never a drawn originator.
2. Attack the new rule:
   - any input that draws a node the input does not name as a whole
   - a whole stored name that is in fact a different product (a data problem; say so, and separate it from code problems)
   - Unicode, symbols and invisible characters that `_sig`/`_latin` mishandle
   - the id path
   - the `choose` reply (live nodes only, injection-safe page markup, click-through)
   - company/target whole-name reads
   - retired nodes
   - anything the page text claims that the code does not do
3. Check P32-5 for regressions in the question boxes (2 to 4) and the demo examples. Report over-refusals as should-fix, not blockers.
4. Re-check guarantees A to G of the original brief briefly; earlier rounds approved them.

### Out of scope as blockers (recorded)

- The question boxes' free-text refusal relies on word lists (README defect 15). A leak there is a note, not a map blocker.
- Known data limitations, awaiting the owner's curation decision:
  - ChEMBL lists bevacizumab biosimilar development codes as bevacizumab synonyms.
  - An AACT synonym maps 13-cis-retinoic acid to CHEMBL38 tretinoin (M23-D1).
  - "Roche" is stored as a folic acid alias. The map now asks the reader to choose.
- Known over-refusals listed in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md` (M5-S2, rapamycin forms, Trastuzumab--vcmmae conjugate, 9-amino-20(s)-camptothecin, il-6ra, TAK-659F, GS-11CAL-101, 3en3hg4wsw, an ADC named with its own payload beside it). A longer input not being drawn is the new rule, not an over-refusal.

Approval bar: no known way for the map to draw a node for an input that is not, as a whole, one stored name or id of it. That includes a refused product drawn as the originator, one of several same-named nodes drawn without the reader's choice, a wrong relation or direction, a value that differs from the stored one, and script injection. Report should-fix items separately from blockers.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m32/`. Put the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m32/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

# Round 34 of the relation-map review

## Before you start

- Read the round-32 brief, `audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`, for the scope, guarantees and approval bar. Read the round-33 report at `audit_evidence_claims/m33/REPORT.md`.
- Repairs are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, section "Round 33".
- Review the latest commit on HEAD.
- Run the tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; expect 362.
- Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>`, on any port except 8765.

## What changed since round 33

The changes are in `src/app.py` `Engine.__init__` and `Engine.explore`, and in `src/query.py`.

- **Drugs are read only by their ChEMBL preferred name.** The map still compares names by `_sig`, as in round 33.
  - Every other stored name of a drug goes into `alias_only`: trade names, ChEMBL synonyms and registry (AACT) synonyms. Typing one returns the reply `alias_only`, and the page asks for the preferred name.
  - This replaces round 33's partial source exclusions, which had the replies `registry_name` and `biologic_synonym`.
  - Why: the round-33 data findings showed that trade names, like synonyms, file combination products, formulations and biosimilars under one molecule.
- **The map also reads:** every node's own label, target symbols, and company and disease keys. The company and disease keys only match input whose `_sig` equals the dictionary key.
- **A refused stored name stays refused, however it is spelt.** The question linker checks every stored name that matches the input's `_sig`, whether that name is read or `alias_only`. If any is refused, the input is refused. This repairs M33-B1: `Myocet™`, `My/o/cet`, `Inf/lectra` and `Megace/ES` are now refused.
- **More names in `_OTHER_PRODUCT_NAMES` (`src/query.py`).** The question boxes now also refuse the round-33 combination products, plus PRED-G, NEO-DELTA-CORTEF and LEP-ETU.
- **The preferred-name rule for a shared key in a question** now needs the name to be written as a word of its own. This repairs M33-S1, "(+/-)NICOTINE".
- **Docs:** README defects 9 and 15 in both languages, and section 0 of `web/try.html`.

## What to do

1. Re-run every round-33 finding (M33-B1, D1–D6, S1, S2) and the round-32 findings. Mark each FIXED or NOT FIXED, with exact inputs. Re-run the historical replays against the map.
2. Attack the design. Look for:
   - any input drawn for a node of which it is not, as a whole, one read name under `_sig`;
   - a read name (a ChEMBL preferred name, a node label, a target symbol, a company or disease key) that denotes a different product from the node drawn;
   - a stored name the question linker refuses that the map still draws under some spelling;
   - Unicode problems, the `choose` and `alias_only` replies and their markup, retired nodes, and the id path.
3. Check the question-linker changes for regressions in the question boxes and the demos.
4. Briefly re-check guarantees A–G.

Report data problems separately from code problems. For each data problem give the exact input, the source record and an independent identity reference.

## Not blockers (recorded)

- Free-text leaks in the question boxes (README defect 15).
- The resolver's own brand lists in graph construction (README defect 15).
- Known over-refusals:
  - the list in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`;
  - brands and development codes that are only synonyms, which the map does not draw;
  - two tracer drugs, refused by their own labels.

## Output

- Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m34/`.
- Put the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m34/REPORT.md`.
- Do not edit tracked files.
- End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.
# Independent relation-map review — round 36

Reviewed **HEAD `c3040c184ad4da0fc638d61ab94d46501c61a11a`**, parent `56eb3d6297f2878d008e5edd9eb5eade62a0841c`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED: two normalization blockers.** The exact M35-B1 and M35-S1 reproductions are fixed. A punctuation character can still hide a negative optical descriptor, and the new descriptor substitution deletes unrelated significant symbols. A separate nested-descriptor question regression is nonblocking under the specified scope. No new upstream data defect was established.

Scope is section 0 of `web/try.html`, `POST /api/explore`, and `Engine.explore`, under the [round-32 approval bar](../m32/brief.md), [round-34 name policy](../m34/brief.md), its [round-35 amendment](../m35/brief.md), and [round-35 report](../m35/REPORT.md). FIXED below closes an exact reproduction under the current preferred-name policy; it does not certify all related spellings, restore excluded aliases, or imply source-data curation. This is a review; no production repair was made.

## Verification

- **`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`: 364 passed in 129.57 seconds**, exit 0; wrapper elapsed 132.828 seconds. One warning came from a subprocess reader decoding UTF-8 output as CP949 in `test_correction_check_fails_when_a_correction_is_pending`. That test was rerun with `PYTHONUTF8=1`: **1 passed in 3.94 seconds**, no warning. The full-suite warning is retained rather than described as a warning-free run. [Full output](pytest.txt), [command/timing](pytest_meta.json), [focused recheck](pytest_warning_recheck.txt).
- Started the actual CLI using **`python src/app.py 8936`**. Root retained **274 HTTP response records**: 124 baseline, 85 exact/adversarial, 36 supplement, 22 new attack cases, and seven Unicode confirmations. This counts retained records, not every request made by all lanes. Empty input and 501-character input produce the two expected baseline HTTP 400 responses. The served page is byte-identical to `web/try.html`. [Baseline](root_confirmation.json), [bodies](root_http.json), [exact cases](root_exact_http.json), [supplement](root_supplement_http.json), [new attacks](root_new_attack_http.json), [Unicode HTTP](ignored_character_http.json).
- **4,689 distinct historical/current inputs**: 301 centers, 1,434 refusals, 2,555 `alias_only`, 327 `not_one_name`, 68 `not_found`, four choices; zero exceptions, independent admitted-name membership failures, silent ambiguous draws, or wrong choice sets. Four changes against the 4,673 prior corpus inputs are intended mark-removal recall: `"trastuzumab\u034f"`, `"trastuzumab\ufe0f"`, `"trastuzumab\U000e0100"`, and `"trastu\u034fzumab"` now draw CHEMBL1201585. Actual Unicode characters are meant in these escaped spellings. [Corpus summary](replay/corpus_summary.json), [responses](replay/corpus_results.json), [parent differences](replay/parent_corpus_changes.json).

## Code blockers

### M36-B1 — underscore punctuation still erases a negative optical descriptor

**Location:** `src/app.py:282-296`, especially `_SIGN_GROUP` at line 289 and the final punctuation filter at line 284. The comment and amended contract say a group with no letters or digits is recognized. Python `\w` also includes `_`, however. `(-_)` therefore does not match; the subsequent punctuation filter deletes both the hyphen and underscore. The sign is never recovered.

| Exact input / Python spelling | Current `_sig` | Fresh HTTP result |
|---|---|---|
| `ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | center **CHEMBL269671** |
| `(-)-ARTEMISININ` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name` |
| `"(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name`; M35-B1 exact repair works |
| `(-_)-ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | **center CHEMBL269671** |
| `(_-)-ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `[-_]-ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(-\u200b_\u0301)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(-\uff3f)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center**; fullwidth underscore folds to `_` |
| `"(-\u203f)-ARTEMISININ"` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name`; undertie punctuation control |
| `CHEMBL269671` | permitted ID path | center CHEMBL269671 |

This is a residual composition defect, present in the parent too, not a claim that the four original M35 inputs still fail. Removing marks/invisibles first correctly repairs those inputs; it does not repair a punctuation character that the descriptor recognizer mistakenly classifies as a word character. [Exact HTTP/code points](root_new_attack_http.json), [step-by-step normalization and parent signatures](normalization_stages.json), [full parent/current comparison](root_parent_comparison.json).

An independent enumeration of **3,501 assigned punctuation/spacing/mark/control characters** whose normalized form is ignorable and contains no additional sign, letter, digit, or grouping delimiter finds **seven failures**: U+005F, U+FE33, U+FE34, U+FE4D, U+FE4E, U+FE4F, U+FF3F. All seven normalize to underscore and all seven draw CHEMBL269671 over HTTP. Unassigned code points, private-use characters, and surrogates are excluded from this count. [Predicate, failures](ignored_character_matrix.json), [HTTP confirmations](ignored_character_http.json), [reproducible checker](root_composition.py).

**Identity evidence:** `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL269671.name` is `ARTEMISININ`, with `aliases[1].alias == "(+)-artemisinin"`; raw provenance is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[768].drug`. ChEBI identifies the natural compound as the positive optical form; primary synthesis research explicitly distinguishes negative artemisinin from its natural positive antipode. These references were reopened during this review. [Source records](focused_source_records.json), [ChEBI](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:223316), [Krieger et al., PMID 29723442](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29723442/).

Thus the source assignment is consistent; the wrong draw is caused by code dropping the input's negative descriptor. The same issue appears with nested empty punctuation, e.g. `(-())-ARTEMISININ`, but the blocker requires only the unambiguous underscore examples above.

### M36-B2 — descriptor substitution consumes unrelated significant symbols

**Location:** `src/app.py:289-296`. The broad pattern accepts any non-word, non-bracket character; `_sign` replaces the entire match with one sign and discards every other symbol it contained. This newly weakens the retained rule that letters, digits and symbols must match (`Engine.explore`, lines 110–115; `_sig`, lines 270–275).

| Exact input | Parent result | HEAD signature / fresh result |
|---|---|---|
| `(+/-)NICOTINE` | CHEMBL440464 | `±nicotine`; same legitimate center |
| `(+/-☢)NICOTINE` | `not_found` | `±nicotine`; **CHEMBL440464** |
| `(±☢)NICOTINE` | `not_found` | `±nicotine`; **CHEMBL440464** |
| `(+/-$)NICOTINE` | `not_found` | `±nicotine`; **CHEMBL440464** |
| `(+/-×)NICOTINE` | `not_found` | `±nicotine`; **CHEMBL440464** |
| `(+/-)NICOTINE☢` | `not_found` | `±nicotine☢`; `not_found` |

The stored preferred name is `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL440464.name == "(+/-)NICOTINE"`. It does not contain ☢ (U+2622, `So`), $ (U+0024, `Sc`), or × (U+00D7, `Sm`). These are neither punctuation nor the ignored trademark marks nor rotation signs. The table's parent/current Engine comparison loads **both parent `app.py` and parent `query.py`**, so this is a demonstrated commit regression rather than only a theoretical regex difference. Root HTTP separately confirms `(±☢)NICOTINE`, `(+/☢-)NICOTINE`, and `(+/×-)NICOTINE`. [Parent/current responses](root_parent_comparison.json), [HTTP](root_new_attack_http.json), [normalization stages](normalization_stages.json), [source record](focused_source_records.json).

The round-36 change summary accurately describes the broad collapse of a sign-containing group with no letters/digits. It does **not** remove the retained approval bar or the explicit guarantee that other significant symbols match. This finding identifies that design conflict: broad group collapse cannot be used to justify dropping arbitrary symbols. No assertion that `☢` alone identifies a particular isotope or pharmaceutical product is needed; the input contains a significant symbol absent from the admitted name.

Across the source inventory, **14 distinct optical-descriptor spellings / 30 source occurrences × six extra-symbol mutations = 84 cases** all lose the extra symbol. Six variants of the admitted `(+/-)NICOTINE` preferred name draw CHEMBL440464. Other source spellings are aliases and stay excluded. [Descriptor source inventory](attacks/descriptor_source_records.json), [all mutations](attacks/sign_symbol_composition.json), [summary](attacks/sign_symbol_composition_summary.json).

## Question-linker regression — nonblocking

### M36-S1 — an extra pair of brackets around the descriptor hides it

**Location:** `src/query.py:118-134`, `_named_alone`. It peels brackets surrounding the name, then requires the adjacent descriptor to match immediately at the edge of the remaining text. It does not look through an extra pair of brackets around that descriptor.

| Exact question | Parent linker | HEAD / question APIs |
|---|---|---|
| `What trials evaluate ((+/-))NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | **CHEMBL3 NICOTINE**, two evidence rows |
| `What does NICOTINE((+/-)) target?` | no entity/evidence | **CHEMBL3 NICOTINE**, two evidence rows |
| `What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?` | CHEMBL3 | no entity/evidence; original M35-S1 fixed |
| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)?` | CHEMBL3 | CHEMBL3; ordinary bracket recall retained |

## Expanded source, historical replay, and guarantees A–G

The expanded sweep covers **22,956 distinct source/key inputs from 34,429 records**, including **every one of the 9,581 entries in `dictionary.drugs[*].aliases`**, all preferred names, index aliases and keys, node labels, target symbols, and company/disease keys. There are 16,015 literal source spellings/read keys and 6,941 additional canonical drug-index key strings. Fresh results: **10,088 centers, 12,486 `alias_only`, 354 refused, eight choices, five `not_found`, 15 `not_one_name`**. Corrected independent membership/refusal/choice assertions have zero failures or exceptions; all responses equal the prior round's corresponding source responses. [Inventory](attacks/all_source_records.json), [source results](attacks/source_sweep.json), [summary](attacks/source_summary.json), [source classes](attacks/source_classified_summary.json), [parent changes](attacks/source_parent_changes.json).

The oracle rebuilds membership from dictionary and SQL records, without reading `Engine.names`. Its first generated version suffered a PowerShell-to-Python encoding error that converted three Unicode literals to question marks. The actual inputs and production replies were unaffected. The original script/results were preserved, the oracle was corrected to ASCII Unicode escapes and checked on four controls, and **all 22,956 saved fresh responses were revalidated**. Only those corrected assertions support this report. [Correction and provenance](attacks/findings.md#oracle-correction-and-limits), [revalidation code](attacks/revalidate_source.py), [revalidation log](attacks/revalidation.log).

Composition was tested on **every stored/source input**, not just the new examples: eight formatting transforms × 22,956 inputs = **183,648 signature comparisons**. Twelve fail, all involving underscore insertion into a stored optical descriptor; the other seven transform families have no differences. Fresh map follow-up shows three excluded optical aliases become drawn preferred names: `(_-_)_-_P_A_C_L_I_T_A_X_E_L` → CHEMBL428647, `(_-_)_-_g_o_s_s_y_p_o_l` → CHEMBL51483, and `(_-_)_-_s_i_r_p_i_g_l_e_n_a_s_t_a_t` → CHEMBL4650367. This demonstrates policy/signature instability; it does not assert a new scientific source misassignment. [All differences](attacks/composition_benign_differences.json), [exact follow-up replies](attacks/composition_benign_map_followup.json).

All 22,956 lose the sign in the second form; **10,084 draw centers and eight produce choices**, while their signed controls remain undrawn. These are repeated manifestations of M36-B1, including company/target/trial names; they are **not 10,084 independently proven chemical identity errors**. ## Standards axis

**Zero hard documented-standard violations identified.** The scoped diff is `git diff HEAD^...HEAD`, commit `c3040c184ad4da0fc638d61ab94d46501c61a11a`, parent `56eb3d6297f2878d008e5edd9eb5eade62a0841c`. No filesystem AGENTS.md or separate coding-style standard was found. The user's supplied evidence-backed review instructions and the round36 brief's no-tracked-edit/scratch-only requirements govern this audit. No unrelated tracker setup or invented style rule was imposed.

The diff preserves parameterized SQL, live-node filtering and page escaping. The new helpers operate on existing strings and introduce no source-data writes, network access or model calls. The map's read-only/no-model statement is `src/app.py:14`; inspected paths retain that behavior. Focused regression cases and comments accompany the changes. Normalization and boundary-condition defects belong to the independently reported Spec axis; passing this Standards check does not establish their correctness.

This audit changes no production code. The user's conditional KOA Studio/KhOpenApi comparison instruction for code repairs is therefore not triggered. Review artifacts, private fixture inputs and temporary files remain under `audit_evidence_claims/m36/`; production integrity is checked separately by hashes. README.md:595 and README.ko.md:404 both state364 tests, matching the requested suite count.

## Spec axis

The exact M35 sign/mark/invisible examples close, but the specified composition is incomplete. The round-36 brief requires a group “with no letters or digits” containing a sign to retain its rotation value. `_SIGN_GROUP` excludes underscore through `\w`; the later punctuation filter therefore erases the negative descriptor in `(-_)-ARTEMISININ`, drawing CHEMBL269671. That is the positive source identity, as supported by its source alias and independent references above. This violates the round-32 approval bar for the whole input naming the drawn node.

The retained `Engine.explore` contract says “every other letter, digit and symbol must match.” `_sign` instead replaces the entire group, including unrelated significant symbols. `(+/☢-)NICOTINE` draws CHEMBL440464 exactly as `(+/-)NICOTINE`. The broadly worded new descriptor prose is in tension with this inherited guarantee; the code follows that broad reduction but fails the retained symbol-preservation requirement. Treat this as a separate significant-symbol loss, not another spelling of the underscore defect.

No unrelated scope expansion or new data misassignment was established in this lane. Passing source membership and refusal sweeps establish behavior for their enumerated inputs; they cannot establish that `_sig` implements the independent composition contract.

Axes remain separate: **Standards: zero hard violations. Spec: two map blockers; a question-only regression is recorded separately.**

## Integrity and limits

All **7,837 checked files**, comprising the tracked-file baseline plus the processed database and resolved dictionary, retain their pre-test SHA-256 values: **zero changed, zero added to the tracked baseline**, tracked git status empty, HEAD unchanged. All scratch files, temporary files and this report are confined to m36. No production repair, source curation, model invocation or external publication occurred. [Initial hashes](integrity_before.json), [final integrity](integrity_final.json).

Root stopped its owned CLI on port 8936 after the replay and question lanes finished using it. All **33 owned/tested ports** are closed: root 8936, contract 9600/9601/9602/9610/9611, and the 27 replay ports. [Root process lifecycle](server_lifecycle.json), [contract lifecycle](contract/summary.json), [replay lifecycle](replay/execution_summary.json), [final link/port validation](report_validation.json).

Opera's connector reported that no browser was connected. Page evidence therefore executes the actual served JavaScript in Node VM DOM/fetch harnesses, including forms, demo callbacks, generated markup and real HTTP click-through. It proves those application behaviors, **not browser painting or layout**. [Browser availability](browser_availability.json), [page assertions](contract/ui_assertions.json).

The source-oracle encoding correction, initial DB audit timeout/retry, and full-suite subprocess-decoding warning are disclosed above with their retained evidence. None is counted as a production defect or silently omitted from the validation account. Passing checks are bounded to the tested inputs and fixtures; no universal free-text refusal, resolver-list correctness, or scientific identity certification is claimed.

The positive checks do not negate M36-B1 or M36-B2. Membership measured only with the implementation's own `_sig` can accept its erroneous equivalence classes. This review separately checks descriptor/punctuation composition and preservation of significant symbols, then confirms the resulting counterexamples through the actual HTTP server.

requirements.txt
---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

 def _named_alone(question: str, name: str) -> bool:
     """The name occurs as a word of its own with no optical-rotation descriptor beside
     it: "NICOTINE" and "(NICOTINE)", but not "(+/-)NICOTINE", "(+/-)(NICOTINE)",
-    "NICOTINE (+)" or a name glued to other letters or signs."""
+    "((+/-))NICOTINE", "NICOTINE (+)" or a name glued to other letters or signs. Any
+    sign in the run of brackets, signs and spaces next to the name counts."""
     for m in re.finditer(re.escape(name), question, re.I):
         before, after = question[:m.start()], question[m.end():]
         if re.search(r"[\w+\-/−±]$", before) or re.match(r"[\w+\-/−±]", after):

+        left = re.search(_DESCRIPTOR_RUN + "$", before).group(0)
+        right = re.match(_DESCRIPTOR_RUN, after).group(0)
+        if _SIGNS.search(left) or _SIGNS.search(right):
             continue                              # a descriptor beside it
         return True
     return False

# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

# Independent relation-map review — round 32

Reviewed **HEAD `b21da2b29c3333af2075433043e32eeb2bb8af21`**, parent `4493fe0f21ad7615594be5d7715b1b90de2a1ed6`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED.** The test suite passes, and the new design prevents the historical longer-input leaks. However, the map can still draw a node for an input that is not a whole stored name, or silently choose one of several live same-name nodes. New exact stored aliases also map different products to the wrong molecule; these are reported separately as data defects.

## Verification and limits

- Ran the requested `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`: **361 passed**. The first run took 48.16s and emitted one Windows cp949 subprocess-reader warning. A justified clean rerun with `PYTHONUTF8=1`, bytecode disabled and pytest/temp paths under m32 produced **361 passed in 43.71s, no warnings**. [Clean output](pytest_utf8.txt), [initial output](pytest.txt). The initial pytest invocation used pytest's default temporary location; subsequent audit scripts, artifacts and final-run temporary files are under m32.
- Started the actual application with **`python src/app.py 8932`** and exercised it over HTTP. The served page exactly equalled `web/try.html`. Root independently captured **50 requests**, including the principal code/data counterexamples, valid ids, refusals and input errors; other lanes retain their own original-CLI captures. [First30](root_http.json), [additional20 plus stored/live-name checks](root_additional_http.json). HTTP200 alone was never treated as semantic success.
- Compared **all 6,469 live centers** against independent SQLite results: **zero errors**, covering 22 relation/direction/type/role combinations. **19 DB assertions and 26 UI/server assertions pass**. All 36 demo HTTP responses equal direct Engine results and the query-only HEAD^ comparison.
- UI checks execute the actual served JavaScript in a **Node VM DOM/fetch harness**, using real HTTP for choices and demos. They verify generated markup, delegated clicks and state transitions; they are **not a painted-browser or visual-layout test**.
- No production code was changed and no model was called. Read paths and `use_llm=False` were inspected; private data mutations use in-memory copies. Final tracked status is clean; all 67 files in the root HTTP baseline, including the database/dictionary, remain byte-identical. That baseline was taken after the initial test run, not before it. The owned port 8932 is closed. [Root HTTP hash check](root_confirmation.json), [final integrity](integrity_final.json), [diff under review](review_diff.txt). The conditional KOA Studio/KhOpenApi before-and-after code requirement is not triggered by this read-only review.

## Code blockers

**M32-B1 — comparing an echoed linker window does not prove a stored-name match.** At `src/app.py:111-115`, `_sig(input)` is compared with `_sig(matched_text)`. But `src/query.py:872-877` supplies the candidate input window after looking it up through the lossy canonical key (`src/textrules.py:340-347`). This proves input-span coverage, not equality to an actual stored alias. `_plain` permits characters the dictionary discards.

`trastu+zumab`, `trastu$zumab`, and `trastu=zumab` all draw **CHEMBL1201585 TRASTUZUMAB**. So do `trastuzumabß`, `trastuzumabø`, `trastuzumabæ`, `trastuzumabł`, `trastuzumabð`, and `trastuzumabı`. The significant extra symbol/letter is absent from every stored alias of the drawn drug. The root independently checked full stored aliases/live nodes for `trastu+zumab` and `trastuzumabß`; neither has a matching name. This violates P32-1 regardless of whether the altered text denotes a real marketed product. [HTTP and alias proof](root_additional_http.json), [broader variants](attacks/http_identity.json), [independent sanity assertions](attacks/sanity_assertions.json).

**M32-B2 — company fallback bypasses significant-name equality.** `src/app.py:120-122` accepts a canonical company key whenever `_plain(text)` is true. It never compares the input with a stored name/key retaining significant symbols. **`AstraZeneca +` -> ORG:astrazeneca**, **`Merck + Co` -> ORG:merckco**, and **`Pfizer$`/`Pfi+zer` -> ORG:pfizer**. `Roche $` draws only the company, whereas `Roche` correctly offers the folic-acid/company choice. This is an independent acceptance path even when the linker-window equality fails. The requested `_latin` implementation is absent from actual HEAD; the reviewed implementation is `_plain`. `Merck & Co` itself is a valid stored-label control, not a reported counterexample. [Independent HTTP](root_http.json), [extra company controls](attacks/http_identity.json).

**M32-B3 — shared-name collection uses inconsistent equivalence rules.** P32-3 requires a reader choice for several live nodes sharing one name, ignoring case/spacing/punctuation. The map's `_label_key` retains punctuation, target fallback is literal lowercase, and drug candidates inherit P32-5's question-only preferred-name selection (`src/app.py:111-125,242-244`; `src/query.py:742-747`). A sweep of155 ambiguous spellings finds six that draw rather than choose:

| Exact input | Live candidates sharing the significant name | Incorrect automatic result |
|---|---|---|
| `ABT-510` / `ABT 510` | CHEMBL386115 and CHEMBL262489 | Each spelling draws its own preferred-name node |
| `MAGE-A3` | CHEMBL2108705 ASTUPROTIMUT-R and ENSG00000221867 MAGEA3 | Draws the drug; `MAGEA3` correctly chooses |
| `ctla-4` | CHEMBL1789844 IPILIMUMAB and ENSG00000163599 CTLA4 | Draws the drug; `CTLA4` correctly chooses |
| `FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE F 18` / `FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE-F18` | CHEMBL2030434 and CHEMBL497613 | Each spelling draws its own preferred-name node |

All candidates were independently checked live in SQL. MAGE-A3 and ctla-4 demonstrate the failure without relying on the drug-preferred-name exception. Duplicate source names are data facts; automatically selecting one is a map-code violation. P32-5 allows preferred-name selection in question linking, but does not waive the map's choice rule. [Complete sweep](attacks/shared_signatures.json), [independent HTTP/SQL confirmation](root_additional_http.json).

**M32-B4 — a supposedly ignored trademark changes identity.** NFKC expands `™` into letters `TM` before `_sig` removes trademark characters (`src/app.py:237-239`). **`Tarceva`/`Tarceva®` draw CHEMBL1079742 ERLOTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE; `Tarceva™` draws CHEMBL553 ERLOTINIB.** The latter uses a separate stored AACT alias `tarcevatm`. That alias explains the lookup but cannot authorize interpreting a literal ignored trademark as significant letters under P32-1. `trastuzumab™` and `AstraZeneca™` instead lose recall; those are should-fix manifestations, not additional wrong-node cases. [Root HTTP](root_http.json), [interpretation checks](attacks/sanity_assertions.json).

A repair needs to establish identity against actual stored names, collect **all live candidates under one consistent name-equivalence rule**, and only then draw or choose. Comparing text with its own extracted window or checking a character class cannot establish this. Trademark handling must precede compatibility expansion. These are review conclusions, not an implemented patch.

The new map accepts the whole alias faithfully, but its source assignment identifies the wrong substance/product. These are outside the explicitly waived bevacizumab biosimilar codes, 13-cis-retinoic-acid and Roche limitations.

The Onivyde label explicitly distinguishes it from other irinotecan products; NCI separately identifies liposomal doxorubicin. [Manufacturer label](https://dailymed.nlm.nih.gov/dailymed/fda/fdaDrugXsl.cfm?setid=c8b58efa-1820-48a4-b70d-62918fc4abfc), [NCI formulation](https://www.cancer.gov/about-cancer/treatment/drugs/doxorubicin-hydrochloride-liposome). |
| M32-D3 | `ropeginterferon alfa-2b` -> **INTERFERON ALFA-2B / CHEMBL1201558** | NCI describes a PEG conjugate, rather than unconjugated interferon alfa-2b. [NCI definition](https://www.cancer.gov/publications/dictionaries/cancer-drug/def/ropeginterferon-alfa-2b). |

Root independently reproduced every table row over HTTP and retained the matching source records and live center. `Onivyde`, `liposomal irinotecan`, `Doxil`, and `liposomal doxorubicin` are refusal controls, showing that another stored spelling of a distinct formulation is drawn. [Root captures](root_additional_http.json), [source records and controls](attacks/http_data_aliases.json), [full analysis](attacks/FINDINGS.md). The 317 candidate aliases in the exploratory screen are **not 317 proven defects**. These selected rows need source curation; another free-text word-list patch would not establish source identity.

Missing original files or script-adaptation limits are described in the replay lane's coverage notes.

Historical coverage includes **123 copied leaf invocations**, then two successful minimal port/import-path reruns resolving two initial harness failures; **4,423 distinct extracted inputs** from 59 source artifacts; and **129 supplementary original-report/Unicode/oversized HTTP requests**. All 13 M31 leaks also have zero evidence in both original question wrappers. P32-5 review covers 99 shared keys and 708 bare/trial/target-question comparisons:80 changes, all removals of earlier drug readings, including the intended medi4736/5-fluorouracil/DHA-paclitaxel repairs. **All 36 demos are unchanged; no newly introduced loss of a correct preferred-name question reading was established.** Inherited should-fix: `NICOTINE` links to CHEMBL3, but `What trials evaluate NICOTINE?` has no entities and zero evidence across ask/structured/check. ## Historical cost ledger

# Historical cost reproductions — M32

Fourteen copied suite executions plus the reused isotope-instrumentation supplement exited0. The original2,000-piece answer was additionally exercised against the root CLI. Each reproduction and its disposition are below. Timings overlapped other audit work, so these are descriptive latencies, not an isolated benchmark or evidence of a new regression. Historical `isolated_` filenames do not imply process/host isolation. No timeout or wrong map is established by these performance checks; residual costs are should-fix items.

The implementation still runs the linker before the one-name test. Inputs over500 characters are tested as the question answer field or directly, not as successful map inputs. Shared API locking means slow question checks can delay valid maps.

| Earlier finding / workload | Disposition | Current evidence |
|---|---|---|
| M5-S3 cubic scan, 250 x tokens / 499 characters | **FIXED** | Each predicate 2,224 calls; 33,880 aggregate input chars. [evidence](contract/cost/perf_current.json) |
| Original plain-answer timeout, 500 x tokens | **FIXED** | Original HTTP reproduction completes, following map succeeds. [evidence](contract/cost/perf_check_http.json) |
| M6/M7-S3 repeated 2,000-piece answer | **FIXED** | HTTP 200 in 0.821s. Direct original stress suite also completes. [evidence](contract/cost/repeat_2000_http.json) |
| M8-S1 cache rollover | **FIXED** | 82/82 fill requests; boundary and retry draw OSIMERTINIB. 8,193-entry instrumentation caps caches at4,096; rollovers4,097/8,193; repeat resolver calls0. [evidence](contract/cost/cache_bound_instrumentation.json) |
| M10 onward, 400 distinct isa pieces | **NOT FIXED** | Inherited residual lock delay: cold 4.403s, queued map 4.335s; HTTP200. [evidence](contract/cost/isolated_unique_cost_http.json) |
| M11 onward, U+FDFA repeated4,000 | **NOT FIXED** | Inherited expansion overhead: cold 4.366s, queued map 4.317s; HTTP200. [evidence](contract/cost/isolated_unicode_cost_http.json) |
| M11 onward, 1,000 punctuation-separated x tokens | **NOT FIXED** | Residual overhead; direct median 1.074s, no error. [evidence](contract/cost/normalization_cost.json) |
| M12 onward / M31-C1, 1,000 isa matches | **NOT FIXED** | Both current and parent retain1,000 cache misses/1,000,000 processed words; HTTP cold 13.066s, queued map 12.997s. [evidence](contract/cost/marker_instrumentation.json); [evidence](contract/cost/isolated_repeated_match_cost_http.json) |
| M13 repeated A33 124i / M16-S1 isotope rescan | **FIXED** | No cost failure; direct median 0.104s. Isotope instrumentation preserves one whole-item scan at125/1,000 repeats. [evidence](contract/cost/repeated_match_instrumentation.json) |

# Independent relation-map review — round 33

Reviewed **HEAD `db1580d758062e6a4948056dc87fa42b2a04bc21`**, parent `b21da2b29c3333af2075433043e32eeb2bb8af21`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED.** The original M32-B1–B4 and M32-D1–D3 reproductions are fixed under the revised source policy. However, the map can draw explicitly refused products when their spelling changes in a way `_sig` ignores. Six separately verified stored trade names also draw only one ingredient of a different, multi-ingredient medicinal product. These are separate code and data findings.

## Code blocker

### M33-B1 — refusal and admission use different equivalence rules

`src/app.py:59-73` includes trade names even when the question linker's `_OTHER_PRODUCT_NAMES` declares them a different product. `Engine.explore` at `src/app.py:139-150` first asks the linker to refuse the input, then admits a stored name through `_sig`. The latter ignores punctuation, spacing and trademark signs. The former depends on token windows and clause boundaries (`src/query.py:788-805,874-896`) and folds trademark signs differently (`src/query.py:117-118`). Thus an empty/non-refused linker result is not proof that the stored name is safe to draw.

All following pairs were independently reproduced over HTTP. Plain controls return `refused`; the variants return `center`:

| Plain, refused input | Exact variant drawn | Incorrect center |
|---|---|---|
| `Myocet` | `Myocet™`, `My/o/cet`, `My ocet` | `CHEMBL359744` — DOXORUBICIN HYDROCHLORIDE |
| `Inflectra` | `Inf/lectra` | `CHEMBL1201581` — INFLIXIMAB |
| `Equidacent` | `Equi/dacent` | `CHEMBL1201583` — BEVACIZUMAB |
| `Fyarro` | `Fy/arro` | `CHEMBL413` — SIROLIMUS |
| `Megace ES` | `Megace/ES` | `CHEMBL1201139` — MEGESTROL ACETATE |

For example, `_sig('Myocet') == _sig('Myocet™') == _sig('My/o/cet') == 'myocet'`. This is not an extra qualifier absent from the stored name. It is a **known refused product admitted through another path**, directly violating the refusal guarantee. `_OTHER_PRODUCT_NAMES` already records that these brands denote other products; no new external product interpretation is needed to establish the code inconsistency.

The systematic sweep covers **38 stored spellings × 12 forms = 456 cases**. **248 forms across 37 stored spellings draw a center**; 201 are refused and seven return an excluded-source reply. This is one failure family, not 248 separate blockers. [Sweep and exact forms](attacks/blocked_brand_variants.json), [counts](attacks/blocked_brand_summary.json), [independent attack HTTP](attacks/http_attack_reproductions.json), [root corroboration](root_confirmation.json).

The necessary invariant is that equivalent spellings of a known refused stored name remain refused when the map admits names under `_sig`. Fixing only a few displayed variants would leave the differing comparison rules in place. No implementation change was made during this review.

## Data blockers, separate from code

The following are **exact stored trade-name records that the map still reads**. They are not registry-only synonyms, biologic development codes, or free-text question leaks. Each response displays the indicated single-ingredient node and its relations as the identity of the named multi-ingredient medicine.

Every source record below has **`source: null`**, **`alias_type: "trade_name"`** in `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, at `dictionary.index[<key>]`. The `null` is preserved literally here: the raw `tradeNames` entries explicitly say `source: "ChEMBL"` inside the OpenTargets-sourced raw file; the normalized record loses that source argument in `src/normalize.py:174-175`, not a literal `source: "ChEMBL"` field. [Exact records](attacks/all_alias_source_records.json), [raw JSON pointers and provenance](attacks/trade_name_raw_provenance.json), [root source/HTTP confirmation](root_data_confirmation.json), [additional root HTTP](root_http.json).

[HPRA product record](https://www.hpra.ie/find-a-medicine/for-human-use/authorised-medicines/details/28877). |

An ingredient's presence in a product does not make the product's whole name an identity alias for that ingredient under this review's distinct-product guarantee. The six rows are individually numbered here but constitute one systemic data class (grouped as D1 in the attack-lane notes). These records need a product-aware curation/admission decision. Keeping them as trusted whole-name aliases still draws the wrong product even if M33-B1 is repaired.

**Candidate not promoted to a finding:** `Acticin` draws TRETINOIN, while an FDA name-review table identifies an Acticin permethrin cream. However, separate historical FDA NDA 20-400 records explicitly call a tretinoin gel Acticin. That counterevidence prevents an unqualified claim that this stored alias is wrong. It is excluded from the blocker count. [Historical FDA statistical review](https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/98/20400_statr_micror.pdf), [pharmacology review](https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/98/20400_phrmr.pdf), [audit counterevidence record](root_identity_counterevidence.json). Other screened but unproven candidates are likewise not counted.

## New should-fix items and question-box checks

**M33-S1 — the preferred-name question repair can select the wrong stereochemical record.** `(+/-)NICOTINE` by itself links and maps to its preferred-name node, **CHEMBL440464**. But **`What trials evaluate (+/-)NICOTINE?`** and **`What does (+/-)NICOTINE target?`** now link **CHEMBL3 NICOTINE**; the parent query module abstained. Symbol/token splitting leaves a shorter `NICOTINE` window, which the new preferred-name selection at `src/query.py:887-893` accepts. The three question boxes use the resulting wrong evidence; the map refuses those full questions as `not_one_name`. This is a newly introduced question-linker regression, reported as should-fix under the given scope. [Parent/current comparison and HTTP](contract/racemic_nicotine_regression.json), [root corroboration](root_shouldfix_confirmation.json).

The **99-shared-key / 708-case** question comparison contains **12 changes**: ten intended preferred-name recoveries and the two racemic-nicotine question regressions. All **36 demo examples** are unchanged and their actual JavaScript callbacks pass. All **38 added product keys**, bare and in two question wrappers, are refused; **114 question-box HTTP calls** return no evidence for that product matrix. Those successful controls do not cover the map's M33-B1 equivalent-spelling bypass. [Shared-key comparison](contract/shared_key_question_comparison.json), [demos](contract/demo_previous_comparison.json), [new product checks](contract/product_questions.json).

**M33-S2 — a permitted source can be hidden by an earlier excluded-source record.** `Darzalex`, `Tepezza`, `Tevimbra`, `Winrevair`, `Privigen` return `biologic_synonym`; `Lmx 4` returns `registry_name`, even though a `trade_name` record is retained in the drug's alias list. The upstream index deduplicates a spelling before the map classifies its source; `Engine.__init__` reads that index rather than all source records. This loses a permitted trade-name source and makes the reply's explanation inaccurate. It is a recall/source-classification defect, not a wrong-node blocker. The screening list of 97 source discrepancies includes intentional curated removals and is **not 97 proven defects**. [Records and actual replies](attacks/alias_storage_discrepancies.json), [root HTTP](root_shouldfix_confirmation.json).

The exclusion predicate is also broader than the antibody/protein wording: `kind != "Small molecule"` includes oligonucleotides, oligosaccharides and Unknown drugs. Exact `PF-3512676`, `Fraxiparine`, and `BPI-9016M` return `biologic_synonym`. This is a source/wording qualification, not an unsafe draw. U+034F and U+FE0F marks have combining class zero and remain significant; U+200B/U+FEFF are ignored. The exercised Unicode differences fail closed where they do not match. [Source and Unicode HTTP](attacks/source_unicode_http.json).

# Independent relation-map review — round 34

Reviewed **HEAD `1f6017e8ae7923d21cfafca64b1285331e0d2c18`**, parent `db1580d758062e6a4948056dc87fa42b2a04bc21`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED: one code blocker.** The original round-33 and round-32 blocker reproductions are closed, but the new refusal check omits stored aliases removed from the lookup index. `Cabozantinib (s)-malate` is refused, while `cabozantinibsmalate` and even `Cabozantinib (s)-malate?` draw its node under the same `_sig`. This violates the explicit requirement that every matching refused stored name remains refused. Separate nonblocking findings concern question recall, chemical punctuation equivalence and source metadata.

The scope is section 0, `POST /api/explore`, and `Engine.explore`, using the round-34 preferred-name policy and [round-32 approval bar](../m32/brief.md). The prior report and repair notes were reviewed. A FIXED disposition closes the exact reproduction under this policy; it does not mean that upstream records were curated or excluded aliases regained recall. No tracked file was edited.

[Raw baseline](root_http.json), [summary](root_confirmation.json), [supplement](root_supplement_http.json). Other lanes have additional HTTP captures.

Independent read-name/id membership, ambiguity and choice-set checks found **zero violations and zero exceptions**. [Summary](replay/corpus_summary.json), [all results](replay/corpus_results.json).

All 1,066 drug node labels and indexed preferred names agree with the dictionary's preferred names. This checks recorded membership, not the scientific identity of every possible spelling. [Summary](attacks/source_summary.json), [results](attacks/source_sweep.json), [records](attacks/all_source_records.json).

[Availability record](browser_availability.json), [UI execution](contract/ui_harness_results.json).

The first source oracle intentionally covered `dictionary.index` plus node labels/keys. Its zero failures are valid for that universe but do not cover every spelling in `dictionary.drugs[...].aliases`. An independent final review challenged that assumption. The expanded omitted-alias scan found the blocker below; nine additional root HTTP requests corroborated it. Structural membership passes must not be read as proof of full stored-refusal consistency.

## Code blocker

### M34-B1 — a refused stored alias omitted from the index escapes the refusal closure

The exact stored alias **`Cabozantinib (s)-malate`** is present in `data/processed/resolved.json:1` at **`dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL2103868.aliases[2]`**, with `type: "synonym"`, `source: "ChEMBL"`. Its raw provenance is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, **`data[563].drug.synonyms[1].label`**. The canonical index keeps only the preferred spelling **`CABOZANTINIB S-MALATE`** for this drug/key. `src/normalize.py:165-171` retains the synonym in the drug's alias list but admits only the first same-drug entry into the index.

`Engine.__init__` at **`src/app.py:61-65`** builds both name tables only from that index. Consequently the stored-spelling check at **`src/app.py:149-154`** never asks the linker about the omitted alias. The ordinary input check still refuses its exact spelling, but other spellings reach the preferred-name admission path.

| Exact HTTP input | Current result |
|---|---|
| `Cabozantinib (s)-malate` | `refused`, label `Cabozantinib -malate`, reason `names a distinct product or tracer, not the originator molecule` |
| `cabozantinibsmalate` | **center CHEMBL2103868 — CABOZANTINIB S-MALATE** |
| `CABOZANTINIB S-MALATE` | **same center** |
| `Cabozantinib (s)-malate?` | **same center** |
| `C a b o z a n t i n i b   ( s ) - m a l a t e` | **same center** |
| `Cabozantinib/(s)-malate` | **same center** |
| `Cabozantinib\u200b(s)-malate` (actual U+200B, not literal backslash text) | **same center** |

Every name in the table has `_sig == "cabozantinibsmalate"`. The correct literal trademark control `Cabozantinib (s)-malate™` remains refused, and direct ID `CHEMBL2103868` draws the requested ID as allowed. [Independent full HTTP/source confirmation, exact code points](root_blocker_confirmation.json), [initial independent discovery](contract/omitted_raw_refusals.json), [full omitted-alias scan](attacks/omitted_alias_refusal_summary.json), [all follow-up probes](attacks/omitted_alias_refusal_probes.json).

The full supplement examines **1,652 omitted alias occurrences / 1,376 unique spellings**. Eighteen omitted spellings are linker-refused; six have an indexed equivalent. Of 48 targeted variants, five draw, all in this one Cabozantinib failure family; the root's additional question-mark variant also draws. These counts are not separate blockers. The initial discovery's question-mark input is recorded literally; no converted trademark is misreported as a trademark bypass.

This is a **code/spec consistency failure**, not a claim that the salt name is a different chemical product or that this linker refusal is scientifically correct. The brief's rule is unconditional for stored refused names, and no Cabozantinib exception appears in the recorded exclusions. Repair the refusal-equivalence source so it includes all stored alias spellings, including canonical duplicates omitted from the lookup index, while preserving preferred-name-only admission. No production repair was made.

Bare `(+/-)NICOTINE` still draws CHEMBL440464. This is repaired by abstention, not restored sentence recall. |
| M33-S2 | **FIXED classification under the revised policy** | `Darzalex`, `Tepezza`, `Tevimbra`, `Winrevair`, `Privigen`, `Lmx 4` all return `alias_only`. Trade-name recall intentionally remains unavailable; the former distinction between permitted trade names and excluded synonyms no longer applies. |

The implementation now constructs admitted/alias indexes at `src/app.py:59-69`, filters live candidates at `src/app.py:134-146`, and checks indexed equivalent spellings before admission at `src/app.py:147-154`. The old source-specific replies have been replaced by `alias_only`. The six combination products and `PRED-G`, `NEO-DELTA-CORTEF`, `LEP-ETU` also refuse in the question controls. **M33-B1's original variants are FIXED, but M34-B1 demonstrates that its broader refusal-consistency family is not fully closed.**

## New code/design should-fix findings

### M34-S1 — ordinary parentheses or brackets suppress a valid question name

**New, nonblocking question recall regression.** `src/query.py:896-898` excludes parentheses and brackets on either side of a shared-key preferred name. Parent/current comparison and real HTTP show:

| Exact input | Parent | HEAD |
|---|---|---|
| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)?` | CHEMBL3, two evidence rows | No entity, zero evidence |
| `What does (NICOTINE) target?` | CHEMBL3, two evidence rows | No entity, zero evidence |
| `What trials evaluate [NICOTINE]?` | CHEMBL3, two evidence rows | No entity, zero evidence |

All three question APIs show the loss. Unwrapped `What trials evaluate NICOTINE?` still returns two rows; `What trials evaluate (osimertinib)?` still returns 12. A stereochemical prefix attached to a name and punctuation enclosing a name need different treatment. The map correctly leaves these full questions undrawn, so this does not fail the map approval bar. [Parent/current evidence and 45 HTTP calls](contract/boundary_question_comparison.json), [root HTTP corroboration](root_supplement_http.json).

### M34-S2 — the agreed punctuation equivalence can erase stereochemistry

**Nonblocking limitation of the specified `_sig` design, not a newly misfiled alias.** Exact `(+)-NICOTINE` and `(+)NICOTINE` draw **CHEMBL440464, `(+/-)NICOTINE`**. The stored preferred-name record and node label are `(+/-)NICOTINE`; the input is admitted because `_sig` at `src/app.py:261-272` drops the slash and hyphen while retaining `+`, giving `+nicotine` for all three strings. The linker itself returns no entity for the positive-enantiomer forms. [Source record, signatures and HTTP](attacks/stereochemical_punctuation_http.json), [independent root HTTP](root_supplement_http.json).

This is a real chemical distinction: ChEBI identifies `(+)-nicotine` as the R enantiomer, whereas its racemate record contains equal R and S amounts. [ChEBI R-nicotine](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:39162), [ChEBI racemate](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:18723). The review does **not** interpret signature equality as chemical equivalence. This review classifies it as nonblocking under the literal `_sig` admission criterion: these inputs satisfy the stated punctuation equivalence and are not linker-refused names. That is a scope judgment, **not an explicitly recorded stereochemistry exception**. A stronger medicinal-identity guarantee would require changing that comparison policy, preserving stereochemical notation or refusing such normalized matches.

## Data findings, separate from code

### M34-D1 — THEOPHYLLINE is incorrectly typed as Protein upstream

**Nonblocking source metadata error.** Exact input `THEOPHYLLINE` / `Theophylline` draws CHEMBL1355736 with `props.drug_type: "Protein"`. Its raw source is **`data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[191].drug`**, containing `id: "CHEMBL1355736"`, `name: "THEOPHYLLINE"`, `drugType: "Protein"`. `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs["CHEMBL1355736"].drug_type`, and the node properties preserve that value. ChEBI independently identifies theophylline as dimethylxanthine, formula C7H8N4O2. [ChEBI identity](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:28177).

The map reproduces the stored value faithfully, and no contrary molecular identity of the named node was established. Therefore this is a curation issue, not a stored-value transcription failure or a wrong-product identity blocker. [Complete source record and response](attacks/theophylline_metadata.json), [HTTP confirmation](attacks/http_attack_reproductions.json).
