 succeeded in 1557ms:
"""A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.

    python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765

A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
click to move on),
then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re
import sqlite3
import sys
import threading
import unicodedata
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T
from query import OncoKG

PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
PORT = 8765


class Engine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.kg = OncoKG()
        # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
        # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
        self.kg.conn.close()
        self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
        self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.dic = self.kg.dic
        self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
        target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
        self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)
        # The names the relation map reads, under one rule (`_sig`). A drug is read only by
        # its ChEMBL preferred name. Every other stored name of a drug (trade names,
        # ChEMBL synonyms, synonyms copied from trial registrations) is where a product
        # gets filed under a molecule it is not: combination products under one
        # ingredient (Tobradex under dexamethasone), formulations (Myocet, LEP-ETU),
        # biosimilars under the reference molecule (Inflectra, CT-P13), and wrong
        # substances (bupivacaine hydrochloride under antimony). Those names are not read;
        # the reply asks for the preferred name. Every node's own label, target symbols,
        # and company and disease keys are read too.
        # Both tables take every stored spelling: the lookup index keeps one spelling per
        # drug and key, and a spelling it drops ("Cabozantinib (s)-malate" beside
        # "CABOZANTINIB S-MALATE") must still be checked for refusal.
        self.names: dict = {}
        self.alias_only: dict = {}
        for did, drug in self.dic["drugs"].items():
            _add_name(self.names, drug["name"], did)
            for a in drug.get("aliases") or []:
                if a.get("type") != "preferred_name":
                    _add_name(self.alias_only, a["alias"], did)
        for hits in self.dic["index"].values():
            for h in hits:
                if h.get("alias_type") != "preferred_name":
                    _add_name(self.alias_only, h["alias"], h["drug_id"])
        for r in self.kg.conn.execute("SELECT id, label FROM node"):
            _add_name(self.names, r["label"], r["id"])
        for t in self.kg.targets.values():
            _add_name(self.names, t["symbol"], t["target_id"])
        # names stored only as dictionary keys (letters and digits): company ids and
        # former names, the disease's synonyms
        self.keyed: dict = {}
        for k, ids in self.kg.companies.items():
            self.keyed.setdefault(k, []).extend(ids)
        for k in self.kg.disease["index"]:
            self.keyed.setdefault(k, []).append(self.kg.disease["disease_id"])

    def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
        out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
        for c in out["components"]:
            d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
            if d:
                c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
                             ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
                c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
                                           for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
                c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
                    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                    (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
                par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
                if par:
                    c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
                        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                        (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
            if c.get("candidates"):
                c["candidate_names"] = [
                    (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
        return out

    def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
        """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.

        `text` is a node id or one stored name, compared under one rule (`_sig`: case,
        accents, spaces, punctuation and trademark signs aside; every other letter,
        digit and symbol must match): a drug's ChEMBL preferred name, a node's own
        label, a target symbol, or a company or disease key. Anything more (a second
        name, a qualifier, a payload, a dye, a sentence) matches no stored name and is
        not drawn; the reply says to enter the name alone. A drug's other stored names
        (trade names, synonyms, registry strings) are not read: they file combination
        products, formulations, biosimilars and wrong substances under a molecule; the
        reply asks for the preferred name. A name the question linker refuses (a
        biosimilar, a reformulation, a tracer) is refused, whether it is the input or the
        stored name the input matched. A name stored for several live nodes is not drawn
        either: they are listed and the reader picks one by id.

        Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the page states: trials by
        start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph, sponsors by trials they
        lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship keeps its role (lead
        sponsor or collaborator)."""
        conn = self.kg.conn
        matched = None
        ref = text.strip()
        if re.fullmatch(r"(?i)(nct|chembl|ensg)\d+", ref):
            ref = ref.upper()                         # an id typed in lower case
        row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                           (ref,)).fetchone()
        if row:
            nid = row["id"]
        else:
            ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
            refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
            hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
            if refused:                       # any refused part: the input is not one plain name
                return {"refused": refused[0]["label"], "why": refused[0]["why"]}
            sig = _sig(text)
            found = dict(self.names.get(sig, {}))
            if sig and sig == T.canonical_key(text):          # nothing the key would drop
                for k in self.keyed.get(sig, []):
                    found.setdefault(k, None)
            ids = list(found)
            choices = []
            for oid in ids:
                lab = conn.execute("SELECT type, label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                                   (oid,)).fetchone()
                if lab and all(c["id"] != oid for c in choices):
                    choices.append({"id": oid, "type": lab["type"], "label": lab["label"]})
            # a stored name the question linker refuses stays refused however the input
            # spells it ("Myocet" written "My/o/cet" or "Myocet\u2122")
            stored = [n for c in choices for n in found.get(c["id"]) or []]
            stored += [n for names in (self.alias_only.get(sig) or {}).values() for n in names]
            for name in stored:
                no = [e for e in self.kg.link_entities(name) if e["type"] == "Refused"]
                if no:
                    return {"refused": no[0]["label"], "why": no[0]["why"]}
            if not choices:
                if found:                     # stored, but its node is retired
                    return {"not_found": text}
                if self.alias_only.get(sig):  # a drug's trade name or synonym
                    return {"alias_only": text}
                return {"not_one_name": text} if hits else {"not_found": text}
            if len(choices) > 1:
                # one name, several nodes ("Roche"): none is drawn in place of the others;
                # the reader picks one by its id
                return {"choose": choices, "text": text}
            nid = choices[0]["id"]
            matched = (found.get(nid) or [None])[0]
        node = conn.execute("SELECT id, type, label, props FROM node WHERE id=? "
                            "AND retired_at IS NULL", (nid,)).fetchone()
        if node is None:                              # a name can outlive its node
            return {"not_found": text}
        rows = conn.execute(
            "SELECT e.id AS eid, e.rel, e.src, e.props AS eprops, n.id, n.type, n.label, "
            "n.props AS nprops FROM edge e JOIN node n "
            "ON n.id = CASE WHEN e.src=? THEN e.dst ELSE e.src END "
            "WHERE (e.src=? OR e.dst=?) AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND n.retired_at IS NULL",
            (nid, nid, nid)).fetchall()
        drug_ids = list({r["id"] for r in rows if r["type"] == "Drug"} | {nid})
        trials = {}
        for i in range(0, len(drug_ids), 500):
            chunk = drug_ids[i:i + 500]
            trials.update(conn.execute(               # live trials only
                "SELECT e.dst, COUNT(*) FROM edge e JOIN node t ON t.id = e.src "
                "WHERE e.rel='EVALUATES' AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND t.retired_at IS NULL "
                f"AND e.dst IN ({','.join('?' * len(chunk))}) GROUP BY e.dst", chunk).fetchall())
        groups: dict = {}
        for r in rows:
            ep, np_ = _obj(r["eprops"]), _obj(r["nprops"])
            item = {"id": r["id"], "type": r["type"], "label": r["label"], "edge_id": r["eid"]}
            role = ep.get("role") if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS" else None
            if r["type"] == "Drug":
                item["trials"] = trials.get(r["id"], 0)
                item["max_phase"] = np_.get("max_phase_overall")
            if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF":
                n = ep.get("evidence_trials")
                # a count the edge does not record stays unknown; 0 is only for sorting
                item["evidence_trials"] = n if _number(n) else None
                item["weight"] = n if _number(n) else 0
                item["sponsor_class"] = ep.get("sponsor_class")
            elif r["type"] == "Trial":
                item.update({k: np_.get(k) for k in ("phases", "status", "title", "start_date", "url")})
                item["weight"] = str(np_.get("start_date") or "")
            elif r["type"] == "Drug":
                item["weight"] = item["trials"]
            elif r["rel"] == "ASSOCIATED_WITH":
                score = ep.get("association_score")
                item["score"] = score if _number(score) else None
                item["weight"] = item["score"] or 0
            else:
                item["weight"] = 0
            if r["rel"] == "ACTS_ON":
                item["action"] = ep.get("action_type")
            if role:
                item["role"] = role
            key = (r["rel"], "out" if r["src"] == nid else "in", r["type"], role)
            groups.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
        out = []
        for (rel, direction, ntype, role), items in groups.items():
            items.sort(key=lambda x: x["label"] or "")              # names A to Z
            items.sort(key=lambda x: x["weight"], reverse=True)     # then by the stated weight
            out.append({"rel": rel, "dir": direction, "type": ntype, "role": role,
                        "total": len(items), "items": items[:per_group]})
        out.sort(key=lambda g: -g["total"])
        props = _obj(node["props"])
        return {"center": {"id": node["id"], "type": node["type"], "label": node["label"],
                           "matched_text": matched,
                           "trials": trials.get(node["id"], 0) if node["type"] == "Drug" else None,
                           "props": {k: props.get(k) for k in
                                     ("max_phase_overall", "drug_type", "phases", "status",
                                      "title", "url", "start_date") if props.get(k) is not None}},
                "groups": out}

    def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
        return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()

    def structured(self, question: str, payload: str) -> dict:
        """Check a structured answer (claims citing edges) and write the answer from it."""
        kg = self.kg
        entities = kg.link_entities(question)
        evidence = kg.retrieve(kg.classify(question, entities), entities)
        res = kg.verify_structured(payload, evidence)
        res["evidence"] = [{"edge_id": e.edge_id, "statement": e.statement} for e in evidence]
        return res

    def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
        """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
        kg = self.kg
        entities = kg.link_entities(question)
        intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
        evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
        checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
                                       vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
                                       linker=kg.link_entities)
        return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
                "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
                              "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
                "evidence_count": len(evidence)}


_MARKS = "®™©℠"          # registered, trade mark, copyright, service mark


def _sig(text: str) -> str:
    """The one rule by which the relation map compares a typed name with a stored one:
    trademark signs dropped before compatibility folding (so "™" never becomes the
    letters "TM"), then case and accents ignored and spaces, punctuation and invisible
    characters dropped. Letters of any script, digits and symbols are kept, so
    "trastu+zumab", "interferon" with a Greek gamma or "trastuzumab" with a radiation sign
    are not "trastuzumab" or "interferon".

    A name with a bracketed group that holds no letter or digit ("(+/-)NICOTINE",
    "(-)-ARTEMISININ") carries an optical-rotation descriptor, whose signs are
    punctuation. Such a name is compared character for character instead: only case,
    accents, spaces and invisible characters aside, and every dash read as "-". So
    "(-)-ARTEMISININ", "(-_)-ARTEMISININ" and "(+/-\\u2622)NICOTINE" match no stored name but
    their own; "(±)" and "(+/-)" are not taken as the same."""
    t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", "".join(c for c in text or "" if c not in _MARKS))
    t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", t.casefold())
    t = "".join(c for c in t if unicodedata.category(c)[0] not in "MC")
    if any(not any(unicodedata.category(c)[0] in "LN" for c in g) for g in _GROUP.findall(t)):
        return "".join("-" if c in _DASH else c for c in t if unicodedata.category(c)[0] != "Z")
    return "".join(c for c in t if unicodedata.category(c)[0] not in "PZ")


# Brackets of every kind (opening, closing and quotation punctuation) and every dash
_OPEN = "".join(chr(c) for c in range(0x110000) if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) in ("Ps", "Pi"))
_CLOSE = "".join(chr(c) for c in range(0x110000) if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) in ("Pe", "Pf"))
_GROUP = re.compile("[" + re.escape(_OPEN) + "]([^" + re.escape(_OPEN + _CLOSE) + "]*)["
                    + re.escape(_CLOSE) + "]")
_DASH = {chr(c) for c in range(0x110000) if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd"} | {"−"}


def _add_name(into: dict, name: str, nid: str) -> None:
    if name and _sig(name):
        names = into.setdefault(_sig(name), {}).setdefault(nid, [])
        if name not in names:
            names.append(name)


def _number(v) -> bool:
    return isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool) and v == v


def _obj(raw) -> dict:
    """Stored props as a dict; anything else (null, a list, broken JSON) as empty."""
    try:
        v = json.loads(raw) if raw else {}
    except ValueError:
        return {}
    return v if isinstance(v, dict) else {}


def make_handler(engine: Engine):
    class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
        def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
            self.send_response(code)
            self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
            self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(body)

        def do_GET(self):
            if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
                self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
            else:
                self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")

        def do_POST(self):
            try:
                n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
                body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
                text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()
                if len(text) > 500:                      # refused, not silently cut
                    raise ValueError("input is longer than 500 characters")
                if not text:
                    raise ValueError("empty input")
                if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check",
                                     "/api/structured", "/api/explore"):
                    return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
                with engine.lock:
                    if self.path == "/api/explore":
                        res = engine.explore(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/resolve":
                        res = engine.resolve(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/ask":
                        res = engine.ask(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/structured":
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:20000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.structured(text, answer)
                    else:
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
                        if not answer:

# Independent relation-map review — round 35

Reviewed **HEAD `56eb3d6297f2878d008e5edd9eb5eade62a0841c`**, parent `1f6017e8ae7923d21cfafca64b1285331e0d2c18`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED: one code blocker.** The exact M34-B1, S1 and S2 reproductions are fixed, but the optical-sign repair runs before removal of invisible characters. An invisible character inside `(-)` makes its minus disappear, admitting a negative optical isomer as an unqualified preferred-name node. A separate question-linker regression is nonblocking under the given scope. No new upstream data defect was established.

Scope: section 0 of `web/try.html`, `POST /api/explore`, and `Engine.explore`, using the [round-32 approval bar](../m32/brief.md), [round-34 brief](../m34/brief.md), [round-34 report](../m34/REPORT.md), and current repair notes. FIXED below closes an exact original reproduction under the present policy; it does not certify every related spelling, restore excluded brand recall, or imply upstream data curation. No tracked file was edited.

- Started the actual CLI with **`python src/app.py 8935`**. Root captured **254 HTTP requests**: 124 baseline, 36 supplement, 85 exact/adversarial, and nine independent optical confirmations. The two expected baseline errors are empty input and 501 characters, both HTTP 400. The served page equals `web/try.html` byte for byte. Other lanes retain additional requests. [Baseline summary](root_confirmation.json), [baseline bodies](root_http.json), [supplement](root_supplement_http.json), [exact cases](root_exact_http.json), [optical confirmation](root_optical_confirmation.json).
- **4,673 distinct historical/current inputs**: 295 centers, 1,434 refusals, 2,554 `alias_only`, 324 `not_one_name`, 62 `not_found`, four `choose`; no errors, independent admitted-name membership failures, silent ambiguous draws, or wrong choice sets in that corpus. Its comparison with all 4,660 prior inputs changes only `CABOZANTINIB S-MALATE` (center → refused), `5 FU` and `pegintron` (`alias_only` → refused). [Corpus](replay/corpus_summary.json), [all replies](replay/corpus_results.json), [parent differences](replay/parent_corpus_changes.json).

## Code blocker

### M35-B1 — an ignored invisible character defeats optical-sign preservation

**Location:** `src/app.py:278-282`, `_sig`. The new regular expressions recognize optical descriptors before the final character filter removes invisible characters and combining marks. An inserted U+200B is not matched by `\s*`; consequently the ASCII minus remains punctuation and is deleted by that final filter. There is no later descriptor pass.

The following are fresh HTTP results. In escaped spellings, `\u200b`, `\ufeff`, `\u2060`, and `\u0301` denote **actual single Unicode characters**, not six literal backslash characters. Complete inputs, responses, source records, and code-point captures are linked below.

| Exact input / reproducible Python spelling | `_sig` | Map result |
|---|---|---|
| `ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | center **CHEMBL269671** |
| `(-)-ARTEMISININ` | `−artemisinin` (U+2212) | `not_one_name` |
| `"(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **center CHEMBL269671** |
| `"(-\ufeff)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(-\u2060)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(-\u0301)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
| `"(\u2212\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name` |
| `(+)-ARTEMISININ` | `+artemisinin` | `alias_only` |
| `CHEMBL269671` | ID path | center CHEMBL269671, permitted ID control |

[Independent root HTTP/source confirmation](root_optical_confirmation.json), [separate HTTP/code-point reproduction](attacks/rotation_http.json), [70-case descriptor mutation matrix](attacks/rotation_mutations.json), [source pointers and summary](attacks/rotation_source_and_summary.json). The matrix has **56 sign-normalization invariance failures and 14 wrong-sign ARTEMISININ draws**; these are variants of one defect, not 14 independent blockers.

**Identity evidence:** `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL269671`, has preferred name `ARTEMISININ`, with `aliases[1].alias == "(+)-artemisinin"`; raw provenance is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[768].drug`, including its first ChEMBL synonym. The live node has the same preferred label. ChEBI identifies artemisinin as the positive optical form. A primary synthesis study explicitly distinguishes synthetic negative artemisinin from its natural positive antipode. [ChEBI](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:223316), [Krieger et al., 2018, PMID 29723442](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29723442/). The source record is consistent with those references; this is a **code normalization defect**, not a newly misfiled synonym.

**Why this is blocking in this round:** the new repair explicitly makes the optical descriptor part of the name, while the retained comparison contract ignores invisible characters and accents. Those rules together require `(-)-ARTEMISININ` and `(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ` to retain the same negative sign. The latter instead draws a different optical form. The exact original M34-S2 examples are fixed; that does not close this composition failure. Round 34 treated punctuation erasure as a nonblocking limitation of the then-prescribed comparison. This round explicitly changes that prescription to preserve optical descriptors. This finding therefore does not silently broaden the review to arbitrary chemical punctuation.

Under the **buggy implementation** of `_sig`, the admitted input does equal the stored signature. A purely signature-membership test consequently passes, including an independently implemented oracle that repeats the same normalization order. The counterexample rests on the two documented transformation requirements and independently established identity, rather than a claimed failure of that narrower membership test. It is not one of the recorded over-refusals, excluded brands/synonyms, tracer exceptions, or free-text question leaks.

The repair needs to make ignored-character/diacritic normalization and optical-descriptor recognition compose correctly before punctuation is discarded, or fail closed when an optical descriptor cannot be interpreted safely. Verification should preserve `−`, `+`, and `±` through invisible/combining-character variants and must not enable additional stored-name admission. No production repair was made.

## Question-linker regression, separate from the blocker

### M35-S1 — wrapping the preferred name hides an adjacent stereochemical descriptor

**Nonblocking question-only regression**, `src/query.py:898-899`. The new boundary expression permits an opening bracket immediately before the preferred-name token and a closing bracket immediately after it. It does not inspect past those brackets for a descriptor. Exact parent/current comparisons show:

| Input | Parent query.py | HEAD query.py / HTTP |
|---|---|---|
| `What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
| `What does (+/-)[NICOTINE] target?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
| `What trials evaluate [NICOTINE](+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |

The bare racemic control `(+/-)NICOTINE` names CHEMBL440464. All three question APIs receive the erroneous CHEMBL3 evidence for these wrapped cases. The map returns `not_one_name`, so this does **not** independently fail the map approval bar. [Expanded parent/current comparison](contract/expanded_question_comparison.json), [summary and HTTP cases](contract/expanded_question_summary.json), [root corroboration](root_exact_http.json).

The expanded comparison covers **six shared preferred names × 26 formats × two question wrappers = 312 cases**, with 110 changed readings (a mixture of intended recall recoveries and descriptor regressions), **zero map centers**. The inherited **99-key / 708-case** comparison and all **36 shipped demos** are unchanged. ## Expanded source and attack coverage

The oracle reconstructs admitted membership from dictionary and SQL records, **not `Engine.names`**. It includes every drug preferred name and alias, every index entry, node label, target symbol, company key and disease key. It additionally probes canonical drug-index keys as inputs. The **22,956 inputs** comprise **16,015 literal stored spellings/read keys** (the prior 14,639 plus 1,376 globally omitted raw-alias spellings) and **6,941 additional canonical drug-index key strings**. All are within the HTTP length limit; maximum length is 231. The complete sweep uses direct `Engine.explore`; selected cases are separately confirmed through HTTP. [Source inventory](attacks/all_source_records.json), [independent oracle](attacks/source_audit.py), [classified counts](attacks/source_classified_summary.json).

Overall results: **10,088 centers, 12,486 `alias_only`, 354 refused, eight choices, five `not_found`, 15 `not_one_name`**, zero failures/exceptions. The literal-source subset yields 9,081 centers, 6,738 `alias_only`, 187 refused, six choices and three `not_found`. Raw-alias spelling occurrence counts and unique-string counts are kept separate. Same-drug index omissions are **1,696 records / 1,420 spellings**; the prior round's global-spelling omission definition yields **1,652 records / 1,376 spellings**. Both contain the same **18 omitted refused spellings**; all **29 source inputs sharing their signatures now refuse**. All stored preferred-name alias/index spellings and live drug labels agree with `drug['name']`; skipping redundant preferred-name entries leaves no actual alternate-spelling gap in this dataset. [Omission closure](attacks/omitted_refusal_summary.json), [source discrepancies/equality](attacks/source_discrepancies.json).

The refusal sweep exercises **182 actual refused stored spellings × 13 forms = 2,366 inputs; all refuse**. A separate **84 canonical keys × 13 = 1,092** probes all remain undrawn: 1,074 refused, 12 `alias_only`, six `not_found`. The latter 18 are terminal-state differences on three synthetic canonical keys, not failures to refuse literal stored names and not wrong-center draws. This distinction corrects the broader variable name `refused_stored_names: 266` in the unclassified summary. [Classified refusal results](attacks/refusal_variants_classified.json), [full matrix](attacks/refusal_variants.json).

**1,066 preferred names × six significant suffixes = 6,396 inputs** draw no center. **54 private retirement cases / 10 assertion groups** pass: both live → choose, one retired → remaining live candidate, all retired → not-found, including lowercase IDs and no retired-Roche fallback to a drug alias. Production DB hash remains unchanged. [Suffixes](attacks/significant_suffixes_summary.json), [retirement results](attacks/retired_private_fixture.json). import ast, json, pathlib, re, subprocess, sys, unicodedata as U, urllib.request
ROOT=pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
OUT=ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m36'
sys.path.insert(0,str(ROOT/'src'))
import app
def save(name,obj): (OUT/name).write_text(json.dumps(obj,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
parent=subprocess.check_output(['git','show','HEAD^:src/app.py'],cwd=ROOT).decode()
tree=ast.parse(parent)
node=next(x for x in tree.body if isinstance(x,ast.FunctionDef) and x.name=='_sig')
namespace={'re':re,'unicodedata':U,'_MARKS':app._MARKS}
exec(compile(ast.Module(body=[node],type_ignores=[]),'HEAD^:src/app.py','exec'),namespace)
old_sig=namespace['_sig']
def stages(t):
    n=U.normalize('NFKC',''.join(c for c in t if c not in app._MARKS))
    d=U.normalize('NFKD',n.casefold())
    stripped=''.join(c for c in d if U.category(c)[0] not in 'MC')
    sign=app._SIGN_GROUP.sub(app._sign,stripped)
    return {'input':t,'nfkc':n,'nfkd_casefold':d,'no_marks_controls':stripped,'descriptor_matches':[m.group(0) for m in app._SIGN_GROUP.finditer(stripped)],'after_descriptors':sign,'result':app._sig(t),'parent_sig':old_sig(t)}
cases=['(-)-ARTEMISININ','(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ','(-_)-ARTEMISININ','(-\u200b_\u0301)-ARTEMISININ','(-\uff3f)-ARTEMISININ','(-())-ARTEMISININ','(+/-)NICOTINE','(+/-\u2622)NICOTINE','(+/-\u00d7)NICOTINE','(+/-)NICOTINE\u2622']
save('normalization_stages.json',[stages(t) for t in cases])
eligible=[]; failures=[]
for i in range(0x110000):
    c=chr(i)
    if U.category(c)[0] not in 'PZMC' or U.category(c) in ('Cn','Co','Cs') or c in '()[]': continue
    folded=U.normalize('NFKD',U.normalize('NFKC',c).casefold())
    folded=''.join(x for x in folded if U.category(x)[0] not in 'MC')
    # Scope: ignored characters whose normalized form has no letter, digit,
    # additional sign, or grouping delimiter. This predicate is independent of regex \w.
    if any(U.category(x)[0] not in 'PZ' or x in '()[]' or U.category(x)=='Pd' for x in folded): continue
    t='(-'+c+')-ARTEMISININ'
    row={'codepoint':'U+%04X'%i,'name':U.name(c,''),'category':U.category(c),'input_escaped':t.encode('unicode_escape').decode(),'normalized_insert':folded,'sig':app._sig(t)}
    eligible.append(row)
    if row['sig']!='−artemisinin': failures.append(row)
save('ignored_character_matrix.json',{'eligible_count':len(eligible),'failure_count':len(failures),'failures':failures,'predicate':'Assigned P/Z/M/C except private use and surrogate, normalized to punctuation/spacing/empty without sign or grouping delimiter'})
rows=[]
for r in failures:
    t=r['input_escaped'].encode().decode('unicode_escape')
    req=urllib.request.Request('http://127.0.0.1:8936/api/explore',data=json.dumps({'text':t}).encode(),headers={'Content-Type':'application/json'})
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req,timeout=180) as resp: result=json.load(resp)
    rows.append(dict(r,response=result))
save('ignored_character_http.json',rows)
dic=json.loads((ROOT/'data/processed/resolved.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))['dictionary']
raw=json.loads((ROOT/'data/raw/drugs.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
source=[]
for did in ['CHEMBL269671','CHEMBL440464','CHEMBL1355736']:
    record=dic['drugs'][did]
    raw_hits=[{'json_path':f'data[{i}].drug','record':r['drug']} for i,r in enumerate(raw['data']) if r.get('drug',{}).get('id')==did]
    source.append({'drug_id':did,'dictionary_path':f'dictionary.drugs.{did}','dictionary_record':record,'raw_records':raw_hits})
save('focused_source_records.json',source)
print(json.dumps({'eligible_count':len(eligible),'failures':failures,'http_wrong_draws':sum('center' in r['response'] for r in rows)},ensure_ascii=False))
# Round 37 of the relation-map review

Read the round-32 brief (`audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`) for scope, guarantees and the approval bar, the round-34 brief for the name policy (`audit_evidence_claims/m34/brief.md`), and the round-36 report (`audit_evidence_claims/m36/REPORT.md`). Repairs: `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md` section "Round 36". Review the latest commit on HEAD. Tests: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (expect 365). Page: `python src/app.py <port>` (not 8765).

## What changed since round 36

- `src/app.py` `_sig` no longer interprets optical-rotation descriptors. After trademark signs, compatibility folding, case, marks (accents) and invisible characters are removed, a name that contains a bracketed group (any Unicode opening/closing or initial/final quotation punctuation) holding no letter or digit is compared character for character: only spaces are dropped and every dash (category Pd, U+2212) is read as "-". Every other name keeps the round-32 rule (spaces and punctuation dropped). Stored names go through the same function, so a descriptor name matches only its own spelling (spaces, case, accents, invisible characters and dash style aside).
- `src/query.py` `_named_alone`: any sign (+, -, minus, plus-minus) in the run of brackets, signs, dashes, slashes, underscores and spaces next to a shared-key name in a question blocks the preferred-name reading.

## What to do

1. Re-run M36-B1, B2, S1 and every round-32 to round-35 finding; FIXED / NOT FIXED with exact inputs. Re-run the historical replays and the expanded source universe.
2. Attack the design as in the round-34 brief, including the two comparison modes and which mode a name falls into.
3. Check the question-linker change for regressions in the question boxes and demos.
4. Re-check guarantees A–G briefly.

Report data problems separately from code problems. Recorded, not blockers: question-box free-text leaks and the resolver's own lists (README defect 15); the over-refusals in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, synonyms and brands the map does not draw, descriptor spellings other than the stored one, two tracer drugs and preferred names written like a refused spelling; THEOPHYLLINE's upstream drug type.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m37/`, the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m37/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

# Computer Use

Use this skill to automate the UI of Microsoft Windows apps. It uses SendInput, UI Automation, and Windows.Graphics.Capture screenshots that work even when windows are occluded.

If this plugin is available, read this entire `SKILL.md` once before Windows automation work, before saying Computer Use is unavailable, and before falling back to other Windows automation.

Start with the directions in the Initialize section below. Read these bundled Markdown files relative to this `SKILL.md` when you need information about the specific topic they cover:

- `../../docs/guidance.md`: core runtime behavior, target-window workflow, screenshot handling, and recovery guidance. You MUST read this before controlling Windows apps.
- `../../docs/api.md`: full `sky` API reference. Read this when you need method signatures or object shapes.
- `../../docs/confirmations.md`: you MUST read this before deciding whether a Windows UI action needs confirmation

## Initialize

The bundled `cua_node` `@oai/sky` package is the core entry point for Computer Use. Import it directly by package name from the JavaScript session. Do not spawn `codex-computer-use.exe`, search for the helper executable, or build a custom helper protocol client.

Run this once per fresh `node_repl` JavaScript session:

```js
if (!globalThis.sky) {
  const { sky } = await import("@oai/sky");
  globalThis.sky = sky;
}
```

"""Grounded question answering over the graph.

The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.

Three properties the answering layer holds to:

* **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
  each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
* **The graph answers, not the model.** With a key, the language model is given
  the retrieved evidence and returns claims, not prose; each claim is checked
  against the graph and the answer is written from the accepted ones in the
  graph's own words (structured.py). With no key the evidence is rendered
  directly.
* **Nothing unsupported is shipped.** A claim the graph does not support is
  reported and left out. An unverifiable answer is worse than a short one.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import functools
import re
import sqlite3
import sys
import unicodedata
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T

DB_PATH = C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite"
TRIAL_URL = "https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/{}"


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# evidence
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
@dataclass
class Evidence:
    statement: str
    edge_id: str
    relation: str
    source: str
    source_record: str | None
    fetched_at: str | None
    confidence: float
    tier: str | None = None
    url: str | None = None
    detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)

    def citation(self) -> str:
        base = self.source_record or self.source
        return f"[{base}]"


@dataclass
class Answer:
    question: str
    intent: str
    entities: list[dict]
    text: str
    evidence: list[Evidence]
    generated_by: str
    checks: dict

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "question": self.question,
            "intent": self.intent,
            "entities": self.entities,
            "answer": self.text,
            "generated_by": self.generated_by,
            "checks": self.checks,
            "evidence": [
                {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
                 "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
                 "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
                 "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
                for e in self.evidence
            ],
        }


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# engine
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Words that list separately named things; a list item ends at one. "with" is not among
# them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
# nanoparticles").
_JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
            "or/and", "and/or"}



# every Unicode dash (category Pd) and minus sign, read as a hyphen
_DASHES = "[" + "".join(re.escape(chr(c)) for c in range(0x110000)
                        if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd" or chr(c) in "\u2212\u207b\u208b") + "]"


def _plain(text: str) -> str:
    """Accents off, as the alias key does."""
    t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
    return unicodedata.normalize("NFC", "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c)))


def _alnum_words(text: str) -> list[str]:
    """Letters-and-digits words, with every other character a separator."""
    return re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(text))


# the brackets, signs, dashes, slashes, underscores and spaces that can make up an
# optical-rotation descriptor beside a name, and the signs themselves
_DESCRIPTOR_RUN = r"[()\[\]{}+\-/_\s−±]*"
_SIGNS = re.compile(r"[+\-−±]")


def _named_alone(question: str, name: str) -> bool:
    """The name occurs as a word of its own with no optical-rotation descriptor beside
    it: "NICOTINE" and "(NICOTINE)", but not "(+/-)NICOTINE", "(+/-)(NICOTINE)",
    "((+/-))NICOTINE", "NICOTINE (+)" or a name glued to other letters or signs. Any
    sign in the run of brackets, signs and spaces next to the name counts."""
    for m in re.finditer(re.escape(name), question, re.I):
        before, after = question[:m.start()], question[m.end():]
        if re.search(r"[\w+\-/−±]$", before) or re.match(r"[\w+\-/−±]", after):
            continue                              # glued to letters or signs
        left = re.search(_DESCRIPTOR_RUN + "$", before).group(0)
        right = re.match(_DESCRIPTOR_RUN, after).group(0)
        if _SIGNS.search(left) or _SIGNS.search(right):
            continue                              # a descriptor beside it
        return True
    return False


def _fold(text: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))



# Mass numbers of the isotopes used in medicine, per element (lower case).
# Radioactive labels only: stable 2H, 13C and 15N mark chemistry ("13-cis"), not tracers.
_MEDICAL_MASS = {"h": {3}, "c": {11, 14}, "n": {13}, "o": {15}, "f": {18},
                 "p": {32}, "s": {35}, "cu": {61, 62, 64, 67}, "ga": {67, 68}, "br": {76, 77},
                 "rb": {82}, "sr": {89, 90}, "y": {86, 90}, "zr": {89}, "tc": {99}, "in": {111},
                 "i": {123, 124, 125, 131}, "sm": {153}, "lu": {177}, "re": {186, 188},
                 "pb": {203, 212}, "at": {211}, "ra": {223}, "ac": {225},
                 # therapy and imaging nuclides whose symbols do not start common words
                 "tb": {149, 152, 155, 161}, "th": {227}, "bi": {212, 213}, "sc": {43, 44, 47},
                 "ho": {166}, "tl": {201}, "xe": {133}, "au": {198}, "pd": {103},
                 "ir": {192}, "ag": {111}, "sn": {117}, "dy": {165}, "pt": {195}}
# Further nuclides, recognised only beside their written-out element name: their symbols
# begin ordinary words ("75 several", "51 criteria"), so a bare symbol would over-refuse.
_NAME_MASS = {**_MEDICAL_MASS, "er": {169}, "se": {75}, "cr": {51}, "co": {57, 58, 60},
              "ce": {134}, "la": {132, 135}, "fe": {59}, "cs": {131, 137}, "mn": {52}}

> audit_evidence_claims\m35\REPORT.md:14:## Code blocker
  audit_evidence_claims\m35\REPORT.md:15:
> audit_evidence_claims\m35\REPORT.md:16:### M35-B1 — an ignored invisible character defeats optical-sign preservation
  audit_evidence_claims\m35\REPORT.md:17:
  audit_evidence_claims\m35\REPORT.md:18:**Location:** `src/app.py:278-282`, `_sig`. The new regular expressions recogn
ize optical descriptors before the final character filter removes invisible characters and combining marks. An inserted
 U+200B is not matched by `\s*`; consequently the ASCII minus remains punctuation and is deleted by that final filter. 
There is no later descriptor pass.
  audit_evidence_claims\m35\REPORT.md:19:
  audit_evidence_claims\m35\REPORT.md:20:The following are fresh HTTP results. In escaped spellings, `\u200b`, `\ufeff`
, `\u2060`, and `\u0301` denote **actual single Unicode characters**, not six literal backslash characters. Complete in
puts, responses, source records, and code-point captures are linked below.

## Expanded source, historical replay, and guarantees A–G

The expanded sweep covers **22,956 distinct source/key inputs from 34,429 records**, including **every one of the 9,581 entries in `dictionary.drugs[*].aliases`**, all preferred names, index aliases and keys, node labels, target symbols, and company/disease keys. There are 16,015 literal source spellings/read keys and 6,941 additional canonical drug-index key strings. Fresh results: **10,088 centers, 12,486 `alias_only`, 354 refused, eight choices, five `not_found`, 15 `not_one_name`**. Corrected independent membership/refusal/choice assertions have zero failures or exceptions; all responses equal the prior round's corresponding source responses. [Inventory](attacks/all_source_records.json), [source results](attacks/source_sweep.json), [summary](attacks/source_summary.json), [source classes](attacks/source_classified_summary.json), [parent changes](attacks/source_parent_changes.json).

The oracle rebuilds membership from dictionary and SQL records, without reading `Engine.names`. Its first generated version suffered a PowerShell-to-Python encoding error that converted three Unicode literals to question marks. The actual inputs and production replies were unaffected. The original script/results were preserved, the oracle was corrected to ASCII Unicode escapes and checked on four controls, and **all 22,956 saved fresh responses were revalidated**. Only those corrected assertions support this report. [Correction and provenance](attacks/findings.md#oracle-correction-and-limits), [revalidation code](attacks/revalidate_source.py), [revalidation log](attacks/revalidation.log).

Composition was tested on **every stored/source input**, not just the new examples: eight formatting transforms × 22,956 inputs = **183,648 signature comparisons**. Twelve fail, all involving underscore insertion into a stored optical descriptor; the other seven transform families have no differences. Fresh map follow-up shows three excluded optical aliases become drawn preferred names: `(_-_)_-_P_A_C_L_I_T_A_X_E_L` → CHEMBL428647, `(_-_)_-_g_o_s_s_y_p_o_l` → CHEMBL51483, and `(_-_)_-_s_i_r_p_i_g_l_e_n_a_s_t_a_t` → CHEMBL4650367. This demonstrates policy/signature instability; it does not assert a new scientific source misassignment. [All differences](attacks/composition_benign_differences.json), [exact follow-up replies](attacks/composition_benign_map_followup.json).

For each of the same 22,956 inputs, the independent optical invariant compares a `(-)-` prefix with `"(-_\u200b\u0301)-"`. All 22,956 lose the sign in the second form; **10,084 draw centers and eight produce choices**, while their signed controls remain undrawn. These are repeated manifestations of M36-B1, including company/target/trial names; they are **not 10,084 independently proven chemical identity errors**. [Composition summary](attacks/composition_summary.json), [signature differences](attacks/composition_sign_differences.json), [draw/control responses](attacks/composition_draws.json).

+import importlib.util, json, pathlib, subprocess, sys, urllib.request
+
+ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+OUT = ROOT / 'audit_evidence_claims/m37'
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / 'src'))
+import app
+
+def save(name, value):
+    (OUT / name).write_text(json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def load_parent(name):
+    dest = OUT / ('parent_' + name + '.py')
+    dest.write_bytes(subprocess.check_output(['git', 'show', 'HEAD^:src/' + name + '.py'], cwd=ROOT))
+    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('m37_parent_' + name, dest)
+    mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
+    sys.modules[spec.name] = mod
+    spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
+    return mod
+
+parent_query = load_parent('query')
+current_query = sys.modules['query']
+try:
+    sys.modules['query'] = parent_query
+    parent_app = load_parent('app')
+finally:
+    sys.modules['query'] = current_query
+old = parent_app.Engine()
+now = app.Engine()
+
+def compact(body):
+    return {'state': next((k for k in ['center','refused','choose','alias_only','not_one_name','not_found','error'] if k in body), 'question'),
+            'center': body.get('center', {}).get('id'),
+            'choices': [c['id'] for c in body.get('choose', [])],
+            'entities': body.get('entities'), 'evidence_count': len(body.get('evidence', []))}
+

+import importlib.util, json, pathlib, subprocess, sys, urllib.request
+
+ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+OUT = ROOT / 'audit_evidence_claims/m37'
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / 'src'))
+import app
+
+def save(name, value):
+    (OUT / name).write_text(json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def load_parent(name):
+    dest = OUT / ('parent_' + name + '.py')
+    dest.write_bytes(subprocess.check_output(['git', 'show', 'HEAD^:src/' + name + '.py'], cwd=ROOT))
+    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('m37_parent_' + name, dest)
+    mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
+    sys.modules[spec.name] = mod
+    spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
+    return mod
+
+parent_query = load_parent('query')
+current_query = sys.modules['query']
+try:
+    sys.modules['query'] = parent_query
+    parent_app = load_parent('app')
+finally:
+    sys.modules['query'] = current_query
+old = parent_app.Engine()
+now = app.Engine()
+
+def compact(body):
+    return {'state': next((k for k in ['center','refused','choose','alias_only','not_one_name','not_found','error'] if k in body), 'question'),
+            'center': body.get('center', {}).get('id'),
+            'choices': [c['id'] for c in body.get('choose', [])],
+            'entities': body.get('entities'), 'evidence_count': len(body.get('evidence', []))}
+
+families = {
+    'M36-B1': ['(-_)-ARTEMISININ', '(_-)-ARTEMISININ', '[-_]-ARTEMISININ',
+               '(-\u200b_\u0301)-ARTEMISININ', '(-\uff3f)-ARTEMISININ', '(-\ufe4d)-ARTEMISININ',
+               '(-())-ARTEMISININ', '\u3014-\u3015-ARTEMISININ', '\u00ab-\u00bb-ARTEMISININ'],
+    'M36-B2': ['(+/-\u2622)NICOTINE', '(\u00b1\u2622)NICOTINE', '(+/-$)NICOTINE',
+               '(+/-\u00d7)NICOTINE', '(+/\u2622-)NICOTINE', '(+/\u00d7-)NICOTINE'],
+    'M36-S1': ['What trials evaluate ((+/-))NICOTINE?', 'What does NICOTINE((+/-)) target?'],
+    'M35-B1': ['(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ', '(-\ufeff)-ARTEMISININ', '(-\u2060)-ARTEMISININ', '(-\u0301)-ARTEMISININ'],
+    'M35-S1': ['What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?', 'What does (+/-)[NICOTINE] target?',
+               'What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+/-)?', 'What trials evaluate [NICOTINE](+/-)?'],
+    'descriptor-controls': ['ARTEMISININ', 'CHEMBL269671', '(+/-)NICOTINE', '( + / - )NICOTINE',
+               '(+/\u2013)NICOTINE', '(+/-)nicotine', '(\u00b1)-nicotine', '(+-)NICOTINE',
+               '(-+)-NICOTINE', '(+/-)NICOTINE\u2622', '()osimertinib', '(_-_)_-_P_A_C_L_I_T_A_X_E_L',
+               '(_-_)_-_g_o_s_s_y_p_o_l', '(_-_)_-_s_i_r_p_i_g_l_e_n_a_s_t_a_t'],
+    'mode-boundaries': ['(((-)))ARTEMISININ', '(a(-))ARTEMISININ', '(a-b)ARTEMISININ',
+               '\u2018-\u2019ARTEMISININ', '\u201c-\u201dARTEMISININ',
+               '[)-ARTEMISININ', '(]-ARTEMISININ', '(-\u200b_\u034f)-ARTEMISININ',
+               '(\u00ae)osimertinib', '(\u2122)osimertinib', '\uff08\uff0b/\uff0d\uff09NICOTINE',
+               '(+/-)N\u00cdCOTINE', '(\u200b+/\u0301-)NICOTINE'],
+}
+
+rows = []
+for family, inputs in families.items():
+    for text in inputs:
+        for route in (['/api/explore','/api/ask','/api/structured','/api/check'] if family in ['M36-S1','M35-S1'] else ['/api/explore']):
+            body = {'text': text}
+            if route == '/api/structured': body['answer'] = '{"claims":[]}'
+            if route == '/api/check': body['answer'] = 'NICOTINE is evaluated in trials.'
+            req = urllib.request.Request('http://127.0.0.1:8937' + route, data=json.dumps(body).encode(), headers={'Content-Type':'application/json'})
+            with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=180) as r: result = json.load(r); status = r.status
+            rows.append({'family':family, 'input':text, 'escaped':text.encode('unicode_escape').decode(),
+                         'route':route,'request':body,'http_status':status,'response':result,
+                         'sig':app._sig(text),'parent_sig':parent_app._sig(text),
+                         'parent_map':compact(old.explore(text)),
+                         'current_linker':now.kg.link_entities(text),'parent_linker':old.kg.link_entities(text)})
+        save('root_round37_http.json', rows)
+summary = [dict(family=r['family'], input=r['input'], escaped=r['escaped'], route=r['route'],
+                current=compact(r['response']), parent_map=r['parent_map'],
+                parent_linker=r['parent_linker'], current_linker=r['current_linker']) for r in rows]
+save('root_round37_summary.json', summary)
+old.kg.conn.close()
+now.kg.conn.close()
+print(json.dumps({'http_records':len(rows), 'families':{k:len(v) for k,v in families.items()},
+                  'unexpected_M36_centers':[r['input'] for r in rows if r['family'] in ['M36-B1','M36-B2'] and 'center' in r['response']]}, ensure_ascii=False))

+## Independent attack on the two comparison modes
+
+The oracle reconstructs names and candidate IDs from the dictionary and SQL records; it does not use `Engine.names`. Its normalization classifier is a separate character scanner rather than the implementation's `_GROUP` regular expression. [Oracle and source sweep](source/source_audit.py), [composition probes](source/modes.py), [boundary probes](source/mode_boundaries.py).
+
+The critical separation is structural: a strict-mode signature retains at least one opening/initial-quotation and one closing/final-quotation punctuation character. An ordinary-mode signature removes all punctuation. Those signature spaces cannot collide. This argument depends on the promised preprocessing order: trademark removal, compatibility/case folding and mark/invisible removal precede classification; space removal and dash normalization cannot erase those retained delimiters. It supports mode separation, not universal chemical equivalence of ordinary punctuation-normalized names.
+
+- **22,956 source/key inputs × 14 transforms = 321,384 comparisons.** Fourteen literal source spellings use strict mode; 22,942 use ordinary mode. No implementation-versus-independent-oracle mismatch was found. There are 42 mode-dependent punctuation-transform differences, which are expected under the new policy. Two additional generic `swapcase()` invariance probes differ for Turkish dotless `ı`; both agree with the prescribed casefold oracle. Those probe assumptions are not silently counted as passing or as new map defects. [Summary](source/source_mode_summary.json), [differences](source/source_mode_differences.json), [follow-up outcomes](source/mode_followup.json).
+- **11,086 assigned punctuation/mark/symbol/space characters × four contexts = 44,344 cases**, plus all **91 opening × 87 closing delimiters = 7,917 pairs**: zero oracle failures or unsafe plain-ARTEMISININ collisions. [Unicode summary](source/unicode_mode_summary.json).
+- **138,557 assigned Unicode letter/number characters** inserted into a candidate group: zero classifier disagreements. Sixteen lose their letter/number category during the specified compatibility/mark preprocessing and correctly change mode afterwards. [Classification results](source/letter_number_classification.json).
+- Nested and mismatched brackets, Unicode quotations, empty groups, fullwidth forms, signs, unrelated symbols, letters/digits inside a group and preprocessing-created empty groups were exercised. **28 HTTP boundary controls** supplement root's confirmations. `(-ARTEMISININ)` and `(-A)RTEMISININ` have letters inside their only group and therefore use ordinary mode, drawing ARTEMISININ; ASCII apostrophes are category Po, not the designated Pi/Pf quotation delimiters. These results follow the explicit new classifier and inherited ordinary punctuation rule. They are disclosed design boundaries, not evidence that the code interprets arbitrary strings as optical descriptors. [Boundary evidence](source/mode_boundary_http.json).
+- **182 literal refused spellings × 13 variants = 2,366, all refused.** A separate 84 synthetic canonical-only keys × 13 = 1,092 cases yield 1,074 refusals, 12 `alias_only`, six `not_found`, no centers. All **1,066 preferred names × six significant suffixes = 6,396** remain undrawn. **54 private retirement cases / ten assertion groups** pass. [Refusal summary](source/refusal_variants_summary.json), [suffixes](source/significant_suffixes_summary.json), [retirement fixture](source/retired_private_fixture.json).

The new adjacent run excludes punctuation such as `.` and symbols such as `☢` and `$`. Looking left from NICOTINE, the scan stops on that character before reaching the sign.

+### M37-S2 — ordinary sign-separated lists lose a shared preferred name
+
+**Question recall/design cost**, `src/query.py:133–135`. `What trials evaluate NICOTINE + osimertinib?` previously linked CHEMBL3 and CHEMBL3353410; now only CHEMBL3353410 remains. The selected evidence consequently changes from two NICOTINE rows to 12 osimertinib rows. `What trials evaluate osimertinib + NICOTINE?` and `What trials evaluate NICOTINE - osimertinib?` also drop NICOTINE. The `/` and `and` controls retain both entities.
+
+This follows the requested rule that **any adjacent sign** blocks a shared-key preferred-name reading. It should be understood as a recall tradeoff, rather than claimed to violate that new rule. All such complete questions remain undrawn by the map. The expanded supplement tests **six names × 30 patterns × two wrappers = 360 cases**, with 74 parent-query changes and zero map centers; not all 74 changes are defects. [Supplement and parent comparison](contract/question_supplement.json), [summary](contract/question_supplement_summary.json), [HTTP](contract/question_supplement_http.json).
+
+Previously existing free-text leaks are separate: `What trials evaluate 〔+/-〕NICOTINE?`, `What trials evaluate NICOTINE«+/-»?` and `"What trials evaluate (+/-)\u200bNICOTINE?"` still read CHEMBL3 with two evidence rows, identically to the parent. These remain recorded question-box limitations. Ordinary `(NICOTINE)` recall and the exact M36 nested-descriptor repair pass.
+
+### M37-S3 — page and explore documentation omit the new punctuation exception
+
+**Documentation should-fix**, `web/try.html:54` and `src/app.py:110–115`. Their unqualified claim that punctuation is ignored no longer describes every name. The new `_sig` docstring explains the exception, but the visible page does not. For example, `(+/-)NICOTINE` draws while `(+/-)NICO/TINE`, `(+/-)NICOTINE!`, or bracket changes do not; `()osimertinib` is also undrawn. Explain that a bracketed group without letters/digits switches the whole name to literal-punctuation comparison. These are accepted recall exclusions, so the wording issue does not negate the map safety result.
+
+## Data problems, separate from code
+

Raw source: `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[191].drug.drugType`; dictionary: `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL1355736.drug_type`. The map faithfully copies it. ChEBI identifies theophylline as dimethylxanthine. +import importlib.util, json, pathlib, subprocess, sys, urllib.request

+ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+OUT = ROOT / 'audit_evidence_claims/m37'
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / 'src'))
+import app
+
+def save(name, value):
+    (OUT / name).write_text(json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def load_parent(name):
+    dest = OUT / ('parent_' + name + '.py')
+    dest.write_bytes(subprocess.check_output(['git', 'show', 'HEAD^:src/' + name + '.py'], cwd=ROOT))
+    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('m37_parent_' + name, dest)
+    mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
+    sys.modules[spec.name] = mod
+    spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
+    return mod
+
+parent_query = load_parent('query')
+current_query = sys.modules['query']
+try:
+    sys.modules['query'] = parent_query
+    parent_app = load_parent('app')
+finally:
+    sys.modules['query'] = current_query
+old = parent_app.Engine()
+now = app.Engine()
+

+            'choices': [c['id'] for c in body.get('choose', [])],

# Round 37 question-linker and wording findings

These are **nonblocking** under the m37 brief: question-box free-text leaks, over-refusals, and descriptor spellings other than the stored spelling are recorded exclusions. None of these cases draws a relation-map center. Parent comparisons load `HEAD^:src/query.py` with the current app and data; this isolates the question-linker change, not a full historical application build.

## Exact M36-S1 — FIXED

`What trials evaluate ((+/-))NICOTINE?` and `What does NICOTINE((+/-)) target?` now return no linked entity and no evidence; the parent returns CHEMBL3 and two evidence rows. The original `(+/-)(NICOTINE)` controls remain blocked, and ordinary `(NICOTINE)` still reads CHEMBL3. Fresh evidence: [question supplement](question_supplement.json) and [summary](question_supplement_summary.json).

## M37-S1 — a punctuation or symbol next to the name hides the descriptor in a question

`src/query.py:118-135`, especially `_DESCRIPTOR_RUN` at line 119 and the edge scans at 132-134, accepts a narrower character set than the parent's `_SIGN_GROUP`. A dot, dollar sign or other unlisted symbol stops the scan before it reaches the sign. The parent recognized such non-word characters inside the bracketed descriptor and abstained.

| Exact question | Parent entities/evidence | HEAD entities/evidence | Map |
|---|---|---|---|
| `What trials evaluate (+/-.)NICOTINE?` | none / 0 | CHEMBL3 / 2 | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate (+/-☢)NICOTINE?` | none / 0 | CHEMBL3 / 2 | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate (+/-$)NICOTINE?` | none / 0 | CHEMBL3 / 2 | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate NICOTINE(.+/-)?` | none / 0 | CHEMBL3 / 2 | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate NICOTINE(☢+/-)?` | none / 0 | CHEMBL3 / 2 | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate NICOTINE($+/-)?` | none / 0 | CHEMBL3 / 2 | `not_one_name` |

The same new readings occur with multiplication sign, colon and comma. All **28 direction/position/control cases** were replayed through **112 HTTP calls**: `/api/ask`, `/api/check`, `/api/structured`, and `/api/explore`. Twelve newly admitted question readings are present. `/ask` and `/check` expose CHEMBL3; `/structured` retrieves its two evidence rows even with an empty claims array. Reverse-position controls such as `NICOTINE(+/-.)` remain blocked because the scan reaches a sign before the dot. `_` controls demonstrate the intended improvement rather than this regression. [First matrix](question_punctuation_regression.json), [mirrored matrix](question_punctuation_mirror.json), [summaries](question_punctuation_summary.json), [mirror summary](question_punctuation_mirror_summary.json).

This is a demonstrated **question-only commit regression**, not a new map blocker or new upstream identity finding. Existing leaks with Unicode brackets or an invisible character, such as `What trials evaluate 〔+/-〕NICOTINE?` and `"What trials evaluate (+/-)\u200bNICOTINE?"` (actual U+200B), were also confirmed; they are identical in the parent.

## M37-S2 — sign-separated independent names lose shared-key recall

The new broad run rule deliberately suppresses CHEMBL3 in `What trials evaluate NICOTINE + osimertinib?`, `What trials evaluate osimertinib + NICOTINE?`, and `What trials evaluate NICOTINE - osimertinib?`. The parent links both drugs; HEAD links only CHEMBL3353410. In the first question the retrieved evidence changes from two nicotine rows to twelve osimertinib rows because the first recognized entity changes. `NICOTINE / osimertinib` and `NICOTINE and osimertinib` preserve both entities. All map inputs remain undrawn.

This is an observed recall cost of the requested design, **not a claim that the implementation violates the new sign-run requirement**. The supplement covers six shared preferred names, 30 patterns and two question templates: **360 cases, 74 parent-query changes, zero map centers**, plus 48 HTTP requests. [Cases](question_supplement.json), [summary](question_supplement_summary.json).

## M37-S3 — the page and explore docstring omit the strict-mode punctuation exception

`web/try.html:54` says punctuation is ignored without qualification, and `src/app.py:110-115` repeats that shorthand. The implemented rule now retains punctuation for names with a letter/digit-free bracketed group. Thus `(+/-)NICOTINE` is admitted while alternate punctuation such as `[+/-]NICOTINE` is not; adding empty brackets to an otherwise admitted name also changes its comparison mode. The repair log accurately describes the exception, but the user-facing explanation should state it too. This is a **documentation should-fix**; excluded descriptor spellings are explicitly nonblocking.

## Baseline questions and demos

The original **36 demos** match fresh HTTP responses and the parent's question implementation exactly. The shared-key baseline contains **99 keys / 708 question and bare-name cases**, with **zero changes**. This does not make the newly constructed descriptor/list questions regression-free. [Demo comparison](demo_previous_comparison.json), [shared-key comparison](shared_key_question_comparison.json).

+## Independent attack on the two comparison modes
+
+The oracle reconstructs names and candidate IDs from the dictionary and SQL records; it does not use `Engine.names`. Its normalization classifier is a separate character scanner rather than the implementation's `_GROUP` regular expression. [Oracle and source sweep](source/source_audit.py), [composition probes](source/modes.py), [boundary probes](source/mode_boundaries.py).
+
+The critical separation is structural: a strict-mode signature retains at least one opening/initial-quotation and one closing/final-quotation punctuation character. An ordinary-mode signature removes all punctuation. Those signature spaces cannot collide. This argument depends on the promised preprocessing order: trademark removal, compatibility/case folding and mark/invisible removal precede classification; space removal and dash normalization cannot erase those retained delimiters. It supports mode separation, not universal chemical equivalence of ordinary punctuation-normalized names.
+
+- **22,956 source/key inputs × 14 transforms = 321,384 comparisons.** Fourteen literal source spellings use strict mode; 22,942 use ordinary mode. No implementation-versus-independent-oracle mismatch was found. There are 42 mode-dependent punctuation-transform differences, which are expected under the new policy. Two additional generic `swapcase()` invariance probes differ for Turkish dotless `ı`; both agree with the prescribed casefold oracle. Those probe assumptions are not silently counted as passing or as new map defects. [Summary](source/source_mode_summary.json), [differences](source/source_mode_differences.json), [follow-up outcomes](source/mode_followup.json).
+- **11,086 assigned punctuation/mark/symbol/space characters × four contexts = 44,344 cases**, plus all **91 opening × 87 closing delimiters = 7,917 pairs**: zero oracle failures or unsafe plain-ARTEMISININ collisions. [Unicode summary](source/unicode_mode_summary.json).
+- **138,557 assigned Unicode letter/number characters** inserted into a candidate group: zero classifier disagreements. Sixteen lose their letter/number category during the specified compatibility/mark preprocessing and correctly change mode afterwards. [Classification results](source/letter_number_classification.json).
+- Nested and mismatched brackets, Unicode quotations, empty groups, fullwidth forms, signs, unrelated symbols, letters/digits inside a group and preprocessing-created empty groups were exercised. **28 HTTP boundary controls** supplement root's confirmations. `(-ARTEMISININ)` and `(-A)RTEMISININ` have letters inside their only group and therefore use ordinary mode, drawing ARTEMISININ; ASCII apostrophes are category Po, not the designated Pi/Pf quotation delimiters. These results follow the explicit new classifier and inherited ordinary punctuation rule. They are disclosed design boundaries, not evidence that the code interprets arbitrary strings as optical descriptors. [Boundary evidence](source/mode_boundary_http.json).
+- **182 literal refused spellings × 13 variants = 2,366, all refused.** A separate 84 synthetic canonical-only keys × 13 = 1,092 cases yield 1,074 refusals, 12 `alias_only`, six `not_found`, no centers. All **1,066 preferred names × six significant suffixes = 6,396** remain undrawn. **54 private retirement cases / ten assertion groups** pass. [Refusal summary](source/refusal_variants_summary.json), [suffixes](source/significant_suffixes_summary.json), [retirement fixture](source/retired_private_fixture.json).
+
+## New nonblocking findings
+
+### M37-S1 — punctuation or a symbol can end the question descriptor scan before its sign
+
+**Question-linker regression**, `src/query.py:118` and `src/query.py:133–135`. The parent accepted arbitrary nonword characters inside a sign group. The new adjacent run excludes punctuation such as `.` and symbols such as `☢` and `$`. Looking left from NICOTINE, the scan stops on that character before reaching the sign.
+
+| Exact input | Parent question result | HEAD question result | HEAD map |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| `What trials evaluate (+/-.)NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |
+| `What trials evaluate (+/-☢)NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |
+| `What trials evaluate (+/-$)NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |
+| `What trials evaluate NICOTINE(.+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |
+
+The same new reading occurs with `×`, `:` and `,`, including mirrored suffix forms. The combined **28-question / 112-HTTP** probe has **12 newly admitted question readings** and no map centers. `/api/ask` and `/api/check` expose the entity; `/api/structured` retrieves the same two evidence rows. The opposite-side punctuation controls remain blocked because the scan meets a sign first. This is a loss of question refusal coverage, not a map admission failure; it is nonblocking under README defect 15 and the explicit review scope. [Prefix/suffix evidence](contract/question_punctuation_regression.json), [mirrored suffix evidence](contract/question_punctuation_mirror.json).
+
+### M37-S2 — ordinary sign-separated lists lose a shared preferred name
+
+**Question recall/design cost**, `src/query.py:133–135`. `What trials evaluate NICOTINE + osimertinib?` previously linked CHEMBL3 and CHEMBL3353410; now only CHEMBL3353410 remains. The selected evidence consequently changes from two NICOTINE rows to 12 osimertinib rows. `What trials evaluate osimertinib + NICOTINE?` and `What trials evaluate NICOTINE - osimertinib?` also drop NICOTINE. The `/` and `and` controls retain both entities.
+
+This follows the requested rule that **any adjacent sign** blocks a shared-key preferred-name reading. It should be understood as a recall tradeoff, rather than claimed to violate that new rule. All such complete questions remain undrawn by the map. The expanded supplement tests **six names × 30 patterns × two wrappers = 360 cases**, with 74 parent-query changes and zero map centers; not all 74 changes are defects. [Supplement and parent comparison](contract/question_supplement.json), [summary](contract/question_supplement_summary.json), [HTTP](contract/question_supplement_http.json).
+
+Previously existing free-text leaks are separate: `What trials evaluate 〔+/-〕NICOTINE?`, `What trials evaluate NICOTINE«+/-»?` and `"What trials evaluate (+/-)\u200bNICOTINE?"` still read CHEMBL3 with two evidence rows, identically to the parent. These remain recorded question-box limitations. Ordinary `(NICOTINE)` recall and the exact M36 nested-descriptor repair pass.
+
+### M37-S3 — page and explore documentation omit the new punctuation exception
+
+**Documentation should-fix**, `web/try.html:54` and `src/app.py:110–115`. Their unqualified claim that punctuation is ignored no longer describes every name. The new `_sig` docstring explains the exception, but the visible page does not. For example, `(+/-)NICOTINE` draws while `(+/-)NICO/TINE`, `(+/-)NICOTINE!`, or bracket changes do not; `()osimertinib` is also undrawn. Explain that a bracketed group without letters/digits switches the whole name to literal-punctuation comparison. These are accepted recall exclusions, so the wording issue does not negate the map safety result.
+

+import importlib.util, json, pathlib, subprocess, sys, urllib.request
+
+ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+OUT = ROOT / 'audit_evidence_claims/m37'
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / 'src'))
+import app
+
+def save(name, value):
+    (OUT / name).write_text(json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
+
+def load_parent(name):
+    dest = OUT / ('parent_' + name + '.py')
+    dest.write_bytes(subprocess.check_output(['git', 'show', 'HEAD^:src/' + name + '.py'], cwd=ROOT))
+    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('m37_parent_' + name, dest)
+    mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
+    sys.modules[spec.name] = mod
+    spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
+    return mod
+
+parent_query = load_parent('query')
+current_query = sys.modules['query']
+try:
+    sys.modules['query'] = parent_query
+    parent_app = load_parent('app')
+finally:
+    sys.modules['query'] = current_query
+old = parent_app.Engine()
+now = app.Engine()
+
+def compact(body):
+    return {'state': next((k for k in ['center','refused','choose','alias_only','not_one_name','not_found','error'] if k in body), 'question'),
+            'center': body.get('center', {}).get('id'),
+            'choices': [c['id'] for c in body.get('choose', [])],
+            'entities': body.get('entities'), 'evidence_count': len(body.get('evidence', []))}
+
+families = {
